Evaluating the ADAU186x Three ADCs, One DAC, Low Power Codec with Audio DSPs 
 
 
PLEASE SEE THE LAST PAGE FOR AN IMPORTANT  
WARNING AND LEGAL TERMS AND CONDITIONS. Rev. 0 | Page 1 of 337 
SCOPE 
This user guide provides a detailed description of the ADAU186x functionality and features, includes ADAU1860 and ADAU1861.  
Note: the HPVDD pin in ADAU1860 is same as the AVDD pin in the ADAU1861. Package differences information referred to it their 
relative data sheets.

Devices for its use, nor any infringements of patents or other rights of third parties that may result from its use. Specifications subject to 
change without notice. No license is granted by implication or otherwise under any patent or patent rights of Analog Devices. Trademarks 
and registered trademarks are the property of their respective owners.

INTRODCUTION TO THE ADAU186X 
The ADAU186x is a low power audio codec with an optimized 
audio processing core, making it ideal for noise cancelling 
applications that require high quality audio, low power, small 
size, and low latency. The three ADC and one DAC channels 
have the SNR of at least 105 dB and the THD + N of at least −90 
dB. The two serial audio ports are compatible with I
2S, left 
justified, right justified, and TDM modes, with tristating for 
interfacing to digital audio data. The analog operating voltage is 
1.8 V , and an optional internal regulator can be used to generate 
the digital supply voltage. If desired, the regulator can be 
powered down and the digital supply voltage can be supplied 
externally which is determined by the REG_EN pin. 
The input signal path includes flexible configurations that can 
accept differential or single-ended analog microphone inputs as 
well as up to four digital microphone inputs. Each input signal 
has its own programmable gain amplifier (PGA) for volume 
adjustment. 
The ADCs and DAC are high quality, 24-bit Σ- Δ converters that 
operate at a selectable 12kHz to 768kHz sampling rate and the 
ADCs also support 8kHz or 16kHz sampling rate in the voice 
wake up mode. The ADCs and DAC have an optional high-pass 
filter with a cutoff frequency of 1 Hz and fine-step digital soft 
volume controls. 
The DAC output is capable of differentially driving a 
headphone earpiece speaker with 16 Ω impedance or higher. 
There is also the option to change to line output mode when the 
output is lightly loaded. 
The Tensilica HiFi-3z core (TDSP) is optimized for low-power 
audio processing. It allows the ADAU186x to provide more 
flexible solutions to meet more complicated applications.    
The FastDSP core has a reduced instruction set that optimizes 
this codec for noise cancellation. The program and parameter 
RAMs can be loaded with custom audio processing signal flow 
built using the Lark Studio. The values stored in the parameter 
RAM control individual signal processing blocks.  
The ADAU186x also has a self-boot function that can load the 
program and parameter RAMs of both cores along with the 
register settings on power-up using an external flash memory 
over the quad SPI interface. The external flash memory is fully 
memory mapped to the HiFi-3z bus fabric.  
The ADAu186x architecture is shown in Figure 2. 
Use the Lark Studio to program and control the cores through 
the control port. Along with designing and tuning a signal flow, 
the tool can configure all ADAU186x registers. The graphical 
user interface (GUI) of the tool allows anyone with digital or 
analog audio processing knowledge to design the DSP signal 
flow and export the flow to a target application. The interface 
also provides enough flexibility and programmability for an 
experienced DSP programmer to have control of the design. In 
ADAU186x development tool, the user can connect graphical 
blocks (such as biquad filters, volume controls, and arithmetic 
operations), compile the design, and load the program and 
parameter files into the ADAU186x memory through the 
control port. The tool also allows the user to download the 
design to an external flash memory for self-boot operation.  
The ADAU186x can generate the internal clocks from a wide range 
of input clocks by using the on-board bypassable fractional PLL. 
The PLL accepts inputs from 30 kHz to 36 MHz. For standalone 
operation, the clock can be generated using the on-board crystal 
oscillator. 
The ADAU186x is provided in a small, 56-ball, 2.980 mm × 
2.679 mm WLCSP package.

SYSTEM CLOCKING AND POWER-UP 
POWER-UP/POWER-DOWN OPERATION AND 
OPTIONS 
When pulled low, the PD pin puts the chip in the lowest power 
state, hardware full chip power-down. If the regulator is being 
used, it also powers down during this state. The chip will fully 
reset in this state and retain no state memory. It’s impossible to 
communicate with the ADAU186x when the PD pin is low.  
By default, out of reset, the chip is in its lowest power state 
(hibernate 2 mode) that can be entered via a control interface, 
software full chip power-down. To enter or exit this power state, use 
the PWR_MODE bits in the CHIP_PWR register. When 
PWR_MODE is 2’b00, the I2C/SPI/UART control ports are 
operational, and everything else is powered down except the 
DVDD regulator, the crystal and the input interrupt pins if used. 
The digital portion of the chips has several power domains. By 
default, only the domain that powers the control ports and their 
associated registers are powered on, the rest of the digital design 
has its power supplies gated and its state is lost.  
There are several options to retain various additional state memory 
during software full chip power down. The SOC_MEM_RETAIN 
and ADP_MEM_RETAIN bits can be used to retain the state of the 
memory in the Tensilica-HiFi-3z core, FDSP and the EQ engine. 
The control register map always retains its state when 
PWR_MODE is 2’b00.  
When PWR_MODE is 2’b00, the CM pin or the common-
mode output can either maintain its state or not by using the 
CM_KEEP_ALIVE bit. If CM_KEEP_ALIVE = 0, the CM 
voltage will be lost when PWR_MODE = “00” , thus producing 
the lowest possible software power-down current. However, if 
CM_KEEP_ALIVE = 0, the ADAU186x will have a longer turn 
on time because the PLL and other analog blocks rely on the 
CM voltage. A wait time of 35 ms is needed for CM to charge 
before any analog blocks, such as the PLL, can be enabled. 
Conversely, with CM_KEEP_ALIVE=1, the power-down current is 
higher, but the start-up time is faster because the 35ms wait time 
can be omitted.   
If CM_KEEP_ALIVE=0, use the CM_STARTUP_OVER register 
to fast charge the CM voltage to have the lowest turn on time. This 
can be done by setting CM_STARUP_OVER=0 before 
PWR_MODE changes from hibernate 2 mode. Then after the 
35ms wait time, set CM_STARUP_OVER=1 to keep power 
consumption low.  The reset state of CM_STARTUP_OVER is 0. 
Therefore, if the 
PD pin is used to power down the part, the step of 
setting to 0 can be omitted.  
When PWR_MODE is not 2’b00, the power supplies on the rest 
of the digital portion of the chip are enabled. Therefore, this 
register must be set first during the power-up sequence.  
The PLL, crystal or frequency multiplier must be configured 
and enabled after CM_STARTUP_OVER sequencing is 
completed.  
After all the internal digital power supplies are powered up, and 
other needed sequencing is completed, the POWER_UP_ 
COMPLETE bit or an interrupt request (IRQ) indicates this. 
The IRQx_POWER_ UP_COMPLETE bit indicates the power 
up complete event. If the IRQs are used to request an interrupt 
after POWER_UP_ COMPLETE, the IRQs must be unmasked. 
The IRQx_POWER_ UP_COMPLETE_MASK bit must be 
cleared. By default, the IRQs for POWER_UP_COMPLETE are 
masked.  
After POWER_UP_COMPLETE=1, the memories can be 
programmed. 
The ADAU186x has highly flexible block level power controls. 
Each individual channel of each block can be powered on or off 
separately. There is a control bit, MASTER_BLOCK_EN, that 
by default is 0 and that overrides all block level gating except 
PLL, XTAL, TDSP and FDSP . The PLL, XTAL, Tensilica core, 
and FastDSP can be enabled, even when MASTER_ 
BLOCK_EN = 0. All other blocks are always in power-down in 
this state, allowing the PLL to be enabled and locked and the 
DSP memories to be initialized before all other signal path 
blocks are enabled.  
When configuring the device, it is recommended to fully set up 
all control registers and block level power controls to their 
desired state, to allow the PLL to lock, to initialize the DSP 
memories to be used, and then to enable the blocks by setting 
MASTER_BLOCK_EN = 1.   
Block level power controls and other settings can be changed 
on-the-fly while the chip is active. However, care must be taken 
when enabling or disabling blocks other than the DAC and/or 
headphone mode blocks that are actively routed out to the DAC 
and/or headphone mode as audible artifacts may occur.  
To power down the chip, set MASTER_BLOCK_EN=0 and 
PWR_MODE= 2’b00. The device then powers down all blocks 
and performs any required power-down sequencing.  
When change the power mode from Active Mode or Hibernate 
0 mode to Hibernate 1 mode or Hibernate 2 mode, which 
means to power off SOC domain,  the SOC domain will be 
powered off directly if the TDSP_W AIT_MODE_BYP bit is set 
to 1, and the SOC domain will be powered off after W AITI 
instruction is executed if the TDSP_W AIT_MODE_BYP bit is 
cleared. 

=== PDF PAGE 13 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 13 of 337 
An overview of the power-up sequencing follows: 
1. Applied AVDD, HPVDD, IOVDD, DVDD and 
HPVDD_L power supplies to ADAU186x following the 
power sequence in Power Supply Sequencing section. 
2. Pull the PD pin high to enable the whole chip and the 
DVDD LDO if used. 
3. There need at least 35ms delay for the CM voltage setting 
before any further operation of the ADAU186x. 
a) If the SELF_BOOT pin is low (ADAU186x works in 
Non-self-boot mode), the external MCU need wait 
at least 35ms after pull high the PD pin and then set 
the STARTUP_DLYCNT_BYP bit to 1.  
b) If the SELF_BOOT pin is high (ADAU186x works in 
self-boot mode), the ADAU186x will count 38282 
clock cycles based on the clock on the MCLKIN pin 
to make sure the CM voltage is ready and then starts 
to download program/data from the external Flash 
through the QSPI interface. The counter can be 
bypassed by setting the STARTUP_DLYCNT_BYP 
bit to 1. Note that the program in the external Flash 
also need to follow the steps below. 
4. Set CM_STARUP_OVER=1 for power saving if need. 
5. Configure the master clock and other frequencies in the 
ADAU186x through the CLK_CTRLx registers and set the 
PLL_EN bit if use. 
6. Configure all other setup bits while the PLL is locking (or 
at any other time after 
E
A pulled high).  PD
7. Ensure that all digital power domains are finished 
powering up, and the clocks are stable by reading the 
PLL_LOCK bit if PLL is used. Verify 
POWER_UP_COMPLETE bit and if this bit is set to 1, 
proceed further or wait until this bit is set to 1.  
DVDD LDO REGULATOR  
There is a DLDO ( DVDD LDO regulator) that can optionally 
generate the DVDD power with using the HPVDD_L supply as 
the DLDO input. If the REG_EN pin is tied to ground, this 
regulator is disabled, and an appropriate DVDD voltage must 
be supplied externally on the DVDD pin. If the REG_EN pin is 
tied to AVDD, the DLDO regulator is enabled and generates 
the required DVDD voltage. If the DLDO is used, necessary 
decoupling capacitors need to be added on the DVDD pins. 
The DLDO_CTRL bits determine the voltage of the LDO 
output as shown in Table 4. By default, the output is 0.9V .  
The DLDO requires the CM voltage to operate. Therefore, even 
if CM_KEEP_ALIVE = 0, the CM output remains present as 
long as the REG_EN=1. Therefore, to achieve the lowest 
possible power-down power when REG_EN = 1, set AAAPD
E
A low. 
Table 4. DVDD Output Setting 
DLDO_CTRL[2:0] Output Voltage of DVDD [V] 
000 0.85 
001 0.9 
010 0.95 
011 1.0 
100 1.05 
101 1.1 
HPLDO REGULATOR 
The on-chip HPLDO regulator is provided as an option for the 
headphone amplifier power supply. A better power supply 
noise will be achieved with using this HPLDO.  
The HPLDO outputs voltage can be controlled with the 
HPLDO_VOUT_SET bits. Customer can disable and bypass 
the HPLDO by using the HPLDO_BYPASS bit and 
HPLDO_EN bit in the HPLDO_CTRL register. 
On power up, the HPLDO will be disabled by default, and the 
headphone amplifier will work with the HPVDD power supply.  
CLOCK INITIALIZATION 
The master clock of the ADAU186x can be generated from the 
PLL, the frequency multiplier or directly from the input source 
frequency. The input frequency source can be selected by the 
PLL_SOURCE bits. 
When a crystal oscillator is used, the crystal oscillator function 
must be enabled in the XTAL_EN and XTAL_MODE bits. Even 
if the crystal oscillator isn’t used, the XTAL_EN bit shall be  
enabled for the system clock consideration. 
If the PLL is used, it must always be set to output clock at 
24.576MHz, 49.152MHz, 73.728MHz or 98.304MHz.  
If frequency multiplier is used, a fixed 24.576MHz clock should 
be provided on the input source frequency pin. 
The PLL and the frequency multiplier can be bypassed by 
setting the PLL_FM_BYPASS bit as 1 and using the input 
source frequency to provide the master clock: providing a fixed 
clock like 24.576MHz, 49.152MHz, 73.728MHz or 98.304MHz 
via the PLL_SOURCE bits and appropriate 
MCLKIN/BCLK_x/FSYNC_x pins.  
All the other clocks in the ADAU186x are derived from the 
master clock and they can be configured though the registers. 
• TDSP_CLK_RATE bits determine the clock of the 
Tensilica-HiFi 3z DSP core 
• BUS_CLK_RATE bits determine the bus clock in the 
Tensilica-HiFi 3z DSP , including the AHB and APB bus 
• FFSRAM_RATE bits determine the clock of the FIFO and 
SRAM 

=== PDF PAGE 14 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 14 of 337 
• UART_CTRL_CLK_RATE bits determine the clock used 
in the UART block. Note that the clock to the UART block 
shall not exceed 49.152MHz. 
• AON_CLK_RATE bits determine the clock for the always 
on blocks in the ADAU186x. It recommends setting the 
AON_CLK_RATE as 0. 
There are some rules need to follow during the clock 
configuration: 
• The clock of the Tensilica-HiFi 3z DSP core shall higher 
than or equal to the bus clock. And the bus clock shall 
higher than or equal to the clock of the FIFO and SRAM. 
• The ratio between the clock of Tensilica-HiFi 3z and bus 
clock shall keep at 1:1, 2:1, 3:1 or 4:1. 
• Bus clock shall be configured at 24.576MHz, 49.152MHz, 
73.728MHz or 98.304MHz. 
The configuration of the MCLK_FREQ_INDEX bits shall 
match with the setting of the master clock, otherwise the 
ADAU186x will not work correctly.  
All internal sampling rates specified within the datasheet is 
48kHz which is based on the assumption that a master clock 
frequency is set at one of the below options: 24.576MHz, 49.152 
MHz, 73.728MHz or 98.304MHz and the 
MCLK_FREQ_INDEX setting matches with the master clock. If 
the master clock is not in the four options, all internal sampling 
rates adjust accordingly. For example, if the master clock is 
32.768 MHz while the MCLK_FREQ_INDEX setting is “01” 
(49.152MHz), all internal sampling rates must be adjusted as 
32.768MHz÷ 49.152MHz × 48 kHz, which is 32kHz. 
The ADAU186x provides clock gating to save power. Each 
function block’s clock can be gated through the appropriate bit 
in the ADP_CLOCK_EN bits. 
If the master clock is higher than 49.152MHz (not include 
49.152MHz), 1.1V voltage should be provided on the DVDD.

PLL Enabled Setup 
To program the PLL during initialization or reconfiguration of 
the ADAU186x, take the following steps: 
1. Ensure that PLL_EN =0 and PLL bypassed. 
2. Set the PLL control registers correctly. 
3. Write 1 to PLL_UPDATE bit to propagate the PLL settings. 
4. Make sure to clear the PLL_FM_BYPASS bit in the 
CLK_CTRL13 register. 
5. Enable the PLL using the PLL_EN bit. 
Other blocks can be powered up while the PLL is not enabled or 
locked. However, if PLL is enabled and not locked, all other 
circuitry will wait until the PLL is locked to begin the power-up 
sequences.  
Control Port Access During Initialization 
Any control registers can be accessed at any time during 
initialization, before PLL is enabled, or during PLL lock.  
To access Tensilica core memories, PWR_MODE[1] must be set 
to 1, and the PLL must be locked, if in use.  
To access FastDSP memories, FDSP_EN must be set to 1, and 
the PLL must be locked, if in use. 
PLL 
The PLL can use any of the BCLK_x, FSYNC_x, or MCLKIN 
signals as a reference to generate the master clock, and the 
source is selected via the PLL_SOURCE bits. Depending on the 
input clock frequency, the PLL must be set for either integer or 
fractional mode. The PLL can accept input frequencies in the 
range of 30 kHz to 36 MHz. PLL_EN bit should be set as 1 if 
PLL is used. 
The PLL output frequency can be set between 24 MHz and 100 
MHz, but the ADAU186x recommends the output frequency of 
the PLL be set at an integer multiple of 24.576MHz like 
24.576MHz, 49.152MHz, 73.728MHz or 98.304MHz.

Figure 4. PLL Block Diagram 
Input Clock Divider 
Before reaching the PLL, the input clock signal goes through an 
integer clock divider to ensure that the clock frequency is 
within a suitable range for the PLL. The 
PLL_INPUT_PRESCALER bits set the PLL input clock divide 
ratio.  
The input frequency limits of the PLL are specified after this 
input pre-scale divider. Therefore, the frequency after division 
must fall within specified range, which is 30kHz to 36MHz. 
Integer Mode 
Integer mode is used when the PLL output is an integer 
multiple of the PLL input clock.  Configuring the PLL_TYPE as 
0 to let the PLL works in the integer mode. 
For example, if the PLL input clock = 12.288 MHz and the 
PLL_INPUT_PRESCALER  = 1, the PLL required output = 
49.152 MHz. Therefore, R = 49.152 MHz/12.288 MHz = 4, 
where R is PLL_INTEGER_DIVIDER.  
Another example is as follows, if PLL input clock = 48 kHz, the 
PLL required output = 49.152 MHz, then R = 49.152 MHz/ 48 
kHz = 1024.  
In integer mode, the values set for N and M are ignored. Table 5 
lists common integer PLL parameter settings for 48 kHz 
sampling rates. 
Fractional Mode 
Fractional mode is used when the clock input is a fractional 
multiple of the PLL output. Setting the PLL_TYPE as 1 to let 
the PLL works in the fractional mode. 
For example, if MCLKIN = 13 MHz and fS = 48 kHz, the PLL 
required output = 49.152 MHz and  
(R + (N/M)) = 49.152 MHz/13 MHz = (3 + (1269/1625))  
where: 
R = 3.  
N = 1269.  
M = 1625.  
Table 6 lists common fractional PLL parameter settings for 48 
kHz sampling rates. When the PLL is used in fractional mode, 
it is important that the N/M fraction be kept within the 0.1 ≤ 
N/M ≤ 0.9 range to ensure correct operation of the PLL.  
When used in fractional mode, the input to the PLL after the 
input divider must be ≥1 MHz.  
FREQUENCY MULTIPLIER 
The frequency multiplier also can be used to provide the master 
clock. Setting the FREQ_MULT_EN as 1 to enable the 
frequency multiplier which will double the input source 
frequency. And the input source frequency is selected via the 
PLL_SOURCE bits. A fixed 24.576MHz should be provided on 
the input source frequency when frequency multiplier is used, 
and which gives a 49.152MHz master clock. 
ADAU186x recommends using the MCLKIN as the source 
frequency when the frequency multiplier is used. 
TRANSITION BETWEEN PLL, FREQUENCY 
MULTIPLIER, AND BYPASS 
The ADAU186x provides three ways to generate the master 
clock: PLL, frequency multiplier and bypass mode (directly 

=== PDF PAGE 16 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 16 of 337 
from the input source frequency). Some procedures need to 
follow when transition between one way to another. 
From PLL to Frequency Multiplier 
When transition from the PLL mode to the frequency 
multiplier mode, below procedures should be followed: 
1. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=1 to bypass the PLL and 
frequency multiplier. 
2. Configure the PLL_EN=0 to disable the PLL. 
3. Configure the FREQ_MULT_EN=1 to enable the 
frequency multiplier. 
4. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=0 and using the 
frequency multiplier to provide the master clock once it’s 
locked. 
From Frequency Multiplier to PLL 
When transition from the frequency multiplier to the PLL 
mode, below procedures should be followed: 
1. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=1 to bypass the PLL and 
frequency multiplier 
2. Configure the FREQ_MULT_EN=0 to disable the 
frequency multiplier 
3. Configure the PLL_EN=1 to enable the PLL 
4. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=0 and using the PLL to 
provide the master clock once it’s locked. 
From PLL/Frequency Multiplier to Bypass Mode 
When transition from the PLL/frequency multiplier to the 
bypass mode, below procedures should be followed: 
1. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=1 to bypass the PLL and 
frequency multiplier. 
2. Configure the PLL_EN=0, or FREQ_MULT_EN=0 to 
disable the PLL or frequency multiplier. 
From Bypass Mode to PLL/Frequency Multiplier 
When transition from bypass mode to the PLL/frequency 
multiplier, below procedures should be followed: 
1. Configure the PLL_EN=1 or FREQ_MULT_EN=1 to 
enable the PLL or the frequency multiplier. 
2. Waiting the PLL lock or the frequency multiplier lock 
3. Configure the PLL_FM_BYPASS=0, using the PLL or the 
frequency multiplier to provide master clock. 
MULTICHIP PHASE SYNCRONIZATION 
Multiple ADAU186x devices can be ensured to remain in phase 
synchronization across the respective audio channels of the 
devices by setting the SYNC_SOURCE bits to use the same 
signal that both chips share. SYNC_SOURCE can be set to 
derive the phase synchronization signal from FSYNC_x. If only 
the shared serial ports between the two ICs are asynchronous to 
the core clock, then the SYNC_SOURCE must use the input 
asynchronous sample rate converter (ASRC). Alternatively, if no 
serial port is used, an internal synchronization source can be 
used. 
CLOCK OUTPUT 
A clock output of varying divisions of the PLL output can be 
generated on any of the MPx pins. The rate of the clock output 
is determined by the MCLKO_RATE bits. 
POWER SUPPLY SEQUENCING 
AVDD, HPVDD, and IOVDD are nominally 1.8 V , and DVDD 
is set at 0.9 V or 1.1V when using the on-board regulator which 
is powered by the HPVDD_L. 
During power-up, AVDD and HPVDD shall be powered up no 
later than other power supplies.  
During power-down, AVDD and HPVDD shall be powered off 
no earlier than other power supplies. 
Enabling the 
PD pin (setting it low) powers down all analog and 
digital circuits and resets the devices to its default state. Before 
enabling the PD pin, mute the outputs to avoid any pops when 
the ADAU186x is powered down.  
The PD pin can be tied directly to IOVDD for normal 
operation. 
Power-Down Considerations 
When powering down the ADAU186x, mute or power down 
the outputs before the power supplies are removed. Otherwise, 
pops or clicks may be heard.

INPUT SIGNAL PATHS 
ANALOG INPUTS 
The ADAU186x can accept both line level and microphone 
inputs. Each of the three analog input channels can be 
configured in single-ended or differential mode with/without 
PGA. There are also inputs for up to four digital microphones. 
The analog inputs are biased at the CM voltage. Connect 
unused input pins to the CM pin or ac-couple the pins to 
ground.   
Phase Difference Various Signal Path in ADAU186x 
Figure 7 shows the phase variation between various blocks 
within the ADAU186x. The gray waveform shows the signal 
path from analog in to digital output or analog output, and the 
black waveform shows the signal path from digital in to analog 
output.  
There is phase inversion from the analog input and the ADC, 
and similarly, from the DAC and headphone outputs (see Table 7). 
However, there is no phase inversion in the digital blocks. 
Input Resistance 
The input resistance of the analog inputs varies with the gain of 
the PGA. This resistance ranges from 1.2 kΩ to 41.2 k Ω with 
different gain setting, input mode configuration and input 
resistance configuration. The resistors inside the ADAU186x 
are precisely matched to each other, resulting in very little gain 
error. However, the exact value of the resistors depends on 
various conditions in the silicon manufacturing process and can 
vary by as much as ±20%. 
With no PGA or line input mode, the input impedance is fixed 
at 9 kΩ in single ended input or 36 kΩ in differential input.  
Analog Differential Inputs 
For microphone signals, the ADAU186x analog inputs can be 
configured with differential input as shown in Figure 6 by 
configuring the AINx_INPUT_MODE bits as “0” . 
The PGA settings are controlled in Register PGAx_CTRLx. The 
PGA is enabled by setting the PGAx_EN bits.

Analog Single-Ended Inputs 
ADAU186x supports single-ended input when 
AINx_INPUT_MODE bits are set to “1” . Figure 8 shows a 
single-ended input using the AINNx pins. The AINPx pin is 
connected to the CM pin or floating.

Figure 8. Single-Ended Line Inputs 
Precharging Input Capacitors 
Precharge amplifiers are enabled by default to quickly charge 
large series capacitors on the analog inputs. Precharging these 
capacitors prevents pops in the audio signal. The precharge 
circuits are powered up by default when an ADC channel is 
enabled and remain on for an amount of time determined by 
the ADC_AIN_CHRG_TIME bits register control. The internal 
impedance for the AINPx, AINNx pins is 750 Ω in this mode. 
However, at startup, the internal impedance is governed by the 
time constant of the reference voltage at the CM pin because the 
input precharge amplifiers use the CM voltage as a reference. 
Programmable Gain Amplifiers 
The PGAs have a programmable gain from 0 dB to 24 dB with 
0.75dB step. The gain is controlled via the PGAx_GAIN 
registers.  
The gain of the PGAs can be configured separately or can be 
linked to the gain setting of the PGA0 when the 
PGA_GAIN_LINK bit set to 1. 
The slew between gain steps is performed automatically when 
the PGAx_SLEW_DIS register is 0. The automatic slew rate can 
be configured by the register bits PGA_SLEW_RATE.  
When the PGAx_SLEW_DIS register is set to 1, the slew can be 
performed manually with the 5 LSBs of the PGAx_GAIN 
register. These bits are intended only for controlling smoother 
transitions between the 0.75 dB steps of the 6 MSBs 
(PGAx_GAIN[10:5]) and must only be set to a 0 when not 
transitioning the gain. 
There are two input resistance mode for the PGAx which can 
be selected through the PGAx_RIN register. When the 
PGAx_RIN is set, the PGAx works in the low input resistance 
mode while the PGAx_RIN is configured as “0”, it works in the 
high input resistance mode. 
The PGAx in the ADAU186x also support different power 
mode to tradeoff the performance and power, which can be 
programmed through the PGAx_POWER_MODE. When the 
PGAx_POWER_MODE is configured as “0”, the PGAx work in 
normal performance mode. When the PGAx_POWER_MODE 
is set to 1, the PGAx works in the enhanced performance mode, 
it achieves high performance with high power consumption. 
The PGAx can work in three mode with combination of the 
PGAx_POWER_MODE and PGAx_RIN: Normal operation, 
Enhanced performance I and Enhanced performance II. 
Table 8.  
PGA Working Mode PGAx_POWER_MODE Bit PGAx_RIN Bit Performance 
Normal Operation 0 0 High input resistance, normal power 
Enhanced Performance I 1 1 Low input resistance, highest power 
Enhanced Performance II 1 0 High input resistance, high power

DIGITAL MICROPHONE INPUTS 
When using digital microphones connected to the DMICx pins, 
the corresponding DMICx_EN registers must be set to enable 
the digital microphone signal paths. The digital microphone 
channels DMIC45 and DMIC67 can be swapped (left/right 
swap) by writing to the DMICxx_EDGE bits. But DMIC0 and 
DMIC2 always sample on rising edge, DMIC1 and DMIC3 
always sample on falling edge. 
DMIC01 or DMIC23 have the option of selecting DMIC0/1 or 
DMIC2/3 share same data or not through setting 
DMICxx_DAT_WIRE bits. But DMIC45 or DMIC67 must 
share same data line. DMIC 45 and DMIC 67 can be set trough 
multi-purpose pins MP0~MP25. 
The digital microphone inputs have two clock sources. 
DMIC_CLKO is outputted from the DMIC_CLK/MP0 pin, 
MP8, MP9, MP10 and MP22 can output inverted DMIC CLK0 
through setting MP_CTRLx. DMIC_CLK1 can be outputted 
from MP7, MP11, MP17, MP22 through setting MP_CTRLx 
The digital microphone data stream must be clocked by the pins 
can output DMIC CLK0 and DMIC CLK1 and not by a clock 
from another source, such as another audio IC. The frequency 
of the DMIC_ CLKx output can be programmed via the 
DMIC_CLKx_RATE bits and should be enabled by setting the 
DMIC_CLKx_EN as “1” . 
Each digital microphone input pair has separate sample rate 
controls that determine the sampling ratio. These controls are 
set via the DMICxx_FS bits. The output sample rate can be set 
between 12 kHz and 768 kHz. The initial decimation filter 
order can be selected between fourth-order or fifth-order via 
the DMICxx_DEC_ORDER bits. The fourth-order selection 
yields the lowest propagation delay, and the fifth-order 
selection may be needed to maintain full performance with 
some high dynamic range microphones. The 
DMICxx_FCOMP bits control whether or not the high 
frequency roll-off of the decimation filter is compensated for. 
No compensation gives the lowest propagation delay but slight 
attenuation in the pass-band. There are separate digital volume 
controls and 1 Hz high-pass filters for each digital microphone 
channel.  
The input pulse density modulation (PDM) is mapped directly 
to the relative pulse code modulation (PCM) full-scale. For 
example, a 50% PDM density input generates a −6 dBFS output 
with a volume control setting of 0 dB.  
The digital microphone signals and the ADCs are completely 
independent and do not share decimation filters. 
Digital DMIC Volume Control 
The volume setting of each digital microphone channel can be 
digitally attenuated in the DMICx_VOL registers. The volume 
can be set between +24 dB and −71.25 dB in 0.375 dB steps. 
The digital microphone volume can also be digitally muted in 
the DMICx_MUTE bits. By default the volume control 
performs a soft ramp when changed, which can be bypassed for 
instantaneous change of volume via the DMIC_HARD_VOL 
bit. The volume control for every channel can be set to use the 
Channel 0 volume via the DMIC_VOL_LINK bit. When a 
digital microphone channel is enabled, it starts immediately at 
the volume level set by its DMICx_VOL register. When a digital 
microphone channel is disabled, it disables immediately and 
does not wait to ramp down the volume.

ANALOG-TO-DIGITAL CONVERTERS 
The ADAU186x includes three 24-bit Σ- Δ analog-to-digital con-
verters (ADCs) with a selectable sample rate of 8 kHz to 768 kHz. 
ADC Full-Scale Level 
The full-scale input to the ADCs (0 dBFS) is nominally 
0.49Vrms in single ended input and 0.98Vrms in differential 
input. Signal levels above the full-scale value cause the ADCs to 
clip. 
In voice wake up mode, The full-scale input to the ADCs (0 
dBFS) is nominally 0.49Vrms in both single ended input mode 
and differential input mode. 
Digital ADC Volume 
The volume setting of each ADC can be digitally attenuated in 
the ADCx_VOL registers. The volume can be set between +24 
dB and −71.25 dB in 0.375 dB steps. The ADC volume can also 
be digitally muted in the ADCx_MUTE bits. By default, the 
volume control performs a soft ramp when changed, which can 
be bypassed for instantaneous change of volume via the ADC_ 
HARD_VOL bit. The volume control for every channel can be 
set to use the Channel 0 volume via the ADC_VOL_LINK bit. 
When an ADC channel is enabled, it starts immediately at the 
volume level set by its ADCx_VOL register. When an ADC 
channel is disabled, it disables immediately and does not wait to 
ramp down the volume. 

=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 21 of 337 
Filtering 
The ADCx_DEC_ORDER bit controls the order of the 
decimation filter. Low order decimation filter gets low delay 
and high order decimation filter gets long delay.  
The ADCx_FCOMP bits control whether the high frequency 
roll-off of the decimation filter is compensated for or not. No 
compensation gives the lowest propagation delay but with 
slight attenuation in the passband. 
A high-pass filter is available on the ADC path to remove dc 
offsets. This filter can be programmed by using the 
ADCx_HPF_EN bits to setting the corner frequency with the 
operation: 1Hz, 4Hz, 8Hz or disable this high pass filter. 
ADC Working Mode 
The ADCx provide a mechanism to trade off the performance 
and power consumption. It can be programmed through the  
ADCx_IBIAS bits as shown in Table 11. 
The ADCx can work in voice wake up mode to further reduce 
power consumption. Set the ADC_VOC_WKUP bit to enable 
the voice wake up mode. During the voice wake up mode, the 
ADCx sample rate can be 8kHz or 16kHz with setting the 
ADC_FREQ bit to 0, and the ADCx data width is 16 bits (16 
MSBs of the ADC output). 
ADC Sample Rate 
The sample rate of the ADCx depends on the ADC_FREQ and 
ADCx_FS bits setting. When the ADC_FREQ sets to 1, the 
ADCx can support sample rate from 12kHz to 768kHz which is 
12kHz based. When the ADC_FREQ configures as 0, the ADCx 
support 8kHz and 16kHz: 8kHz with ADCx_FS is “01” and 
16kHz with ADCx_FS is “10” . 
 
 

=== PDF PAGE 22 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 22 of 337 
OUTPUT SIGNAL PATHS 
Data can be routed to the output DAC path from the serial 
ports, the Tensilica core, the Fast DSP core, the ADCs, the 
digital microphones, the input ASRCs, the EQ, or the 
interpolator. 
The analog output pins are capable of driving headphone or 
earpiece speakers. The line output can drive a load of at least 10 
kΩ or can be put into headphone mode to drive headphones or 
earpiece speakers. The analog output pins are biased at the CM 
voltage. 
ANALOG OUTPUTS 
Headphone Output 
The headphone output is differential, available at HPOUTP and 
HPOUTN. The output pins can be set as a headphone driver by 
configuring the HP_MODE bit to 0 in the HP_CTRL register. 
The headphone outputs can drive a minimum load of at least 10 
Ω. To mute or unmute the headphone output, use the 
DAC_MUTE bit.   
The Headphone can be operated in different performance level 
with different power consumption, which can be configured 
through the PB_POWER_CTRL bits. 
Line Outputs 
Set the output to line output mode by setting the HP_MODE 
bits to 1. The analog output pins (HPOUTP , HPOUTN) can 
drive differential loads of ≥10 k Ω. To mute or unmute the line 
output, use the DAC_MUTE bits 
Headphone Output Power Supply 
The headphone output stage of the ADAU186x can be powered 
by either HPVDD or HPVDD_L.  
When HPVDD provides the power, the ADAU186x works in 
the headphone normal voltage mode and the HPVDD is fixed 
at 1.8V . 
When HPVDD_L provides the power, the ADAU1850 works in 
the headphone low voltage mode and the HPVDD_L support 
voltage between 1.2V to the voltage no more than the HPVDD. 
Follow the below procedure when the transition is happened 
from the headphone normal voltage mode to headphone low 
voltage mode. 
1. Setting the HP_LVMODE_EN=1 to enable the related 
circuit that works in low voltage mode. 
2. Configure the HP_LVMODE_CM_DLY bits to make sure 
the common voltage on the headphone output is stable 
before switch the power stage. 
3. Setting the HP_LVMODE_CM_EN=1 to enable the 
HPVDD_L/2 generation circuit, which will be used as the 
headphone output common voltage in the low voltage 
mode. 
4. Configure the HP_LVMODE_AUTO_DLY bits to set the 
delay time between HP_LVMODE_SWITCH change and 
unmute the DAC output. 
5. Configure the HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE. 
a) If HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE=1: once the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=1, the ADAU186x 
automatically transition to the low voltage mode. 
b) If HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE=0: User need 
to mute the DAC output before setting the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=1. After setting the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=1 and waiting for a delay 
time (need longer than HP_LVMODE_CM_DLY+ 
HP_LVMODE_AUTO_DLY), user need to unmute 
the DAC output. 
6. Setting the HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=1, switch the 
headphone power from the HPVDD to HPVDD_L. 
If the transition is from the headphone low voltage mode to 
headphone normal voltage mode, follow the below steps: 
1. Configure the HP_LVMODE_CM_DLY bits to make sure 
the common voltage on the headphone output is stable 
before unmuting the DAC output. 
2. Configure the HP_LVMODE_AUTO_DLY bits to set the 
delay time between HP_LVMODE_SWITCH change and 
switch the common voltage. 
3. Configure the HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE. 
a) If HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE=1: once the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=0, the ADAU186x 
automatically transition to the normal voltage mode. 
b) If HP_LVMODE_AUTOSW_MODE=0: User need 
to mute the DAC output before setting the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=0. After setting the 
HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=0 and waiting for a delay 
time (need longer than HP_LVMODE_CM_DLY+ 
HP_LVMODE_AUTO_DLY), user need to unmute 
the DAC output. 
4. Setting the HP_LVMODE_SWITCH=0 to switch the 
headphone power from the HPVDD_L to HPVDD. 
5. Setting the HP_LVMODE_EN=0 to disable the related 
circuit that works in low voltage mode. 
6. Setting the HP_LVMODE_CM_EN=0 to disable the 
HPVDD_L/2 generation circuit. 
There is an internal LDO on the HPVDD_L, which gives an 
option to use this internal LDO to provide the clean power for 
the headphone output stage.  
In the headphone normal voltage mode, set the HPLDO_EN=0 
and HPLDO_BYPASS=0 to disable this LDO, the HPVDD 
powers the headphone output stage. 

=== PDF PAGE 23 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 23 of 337 
In the headphone low voltage mode, if configure the 
HPLDO_EN=1 and HPLDO_BYPASS=0 to enable the internal 
LDO, the internal LDO powers the headphone output stage and 
the output voltage of the internal LDO is determined by the 
HPLDO_VOUT.  
If configure the HPLDO_EN = 0 to power down the HPLDO 
and configure the HPLDO_BYPASS=1 to bypass the LDO, the 
HPVDD_L will power the headphone output stage directly. 
Pop and Click Suppression 
To avoid clicks and pops, mute all analog outputs that are in use 
while changing any register settings that may affect the signal 
path. These outputs can then be unmuted after the changes 
have been made. 
DIGITAL-TO-ANALOG CONVERTERS 
The ADAU186x includes one 24-bit, Σ -Δ DAC. This converter 
can operate with input sampling frequencies of 12 kHz, 24 kHz, 
48 kHz, 96 kHz, 192 kHz, 384 kHz, or 768 kHz. The sample rate 
is selectable via the DAC_FS bit.  
DAC Full-Scale Level 
The full-scale output from the DAC (0 dBFS) is nominally 1 V rms 
for a differential output. 
Digital DAC Volume Control and Filtering 
The volume of the DAC channel can be digitally attenuated 
using the DAC_VOL register. The volume can be set to be 
between +24 dB and −71.25 dB in 0.375 dB steps. The DAC 
volume can also be digitally muted in the DAC_MUTE bit. By 
default, the volume control performs a soft ramp when 
changed, which can be bypassed for instantaneous change of 
volume via the DAC_HARD_VOL bit. When the DAC channel 
is enabled, it starts at the lowest volume setting and ramps, if 
DAC_HARD_VOL = 0, to the volume level set by the 
DAC_VOL register. When the DAC channel is disabled, it 
ramps the volume from its current setting, if 
DAC_HARD_VOL = 0, to mute and then turns off.  
A high-pass filter is available on the DAC path to remove dc 
offsets. This filter can be enabled or disabled using the 
DAC_HPF_EN bits. The corner frequency of this high-pass 
filter is set to 1 Hz.  
The DAC_FCOMP bit control whether the high frequency roll-
off of the decimation filter is compensated for or not. No 
compensation gives the lowest propagation delay but with slight 
attenuation in the passband. 
The DAC linear interpolation filter can be selected via the 
DAC_MORE_FILT bit. Setting DAC_ MORE_FILT = 0 results 
in lower propagation delay at the expense of lower attenuation 
of out of band components. 
DAC working mode 
When DAC is working in the normal mode that 
PB_POWER_CTRL bits is 2’b00, there are another two special 
mode called low power mode (LPM) and low power mode Ⅱ
(LPM_Ⅱ) can be used to trade off the DAC performance and 
power. When DAC is working in the enhanced mode that 
PB_POWER_CTRL bits is 2’b10, the low power mode and low 
power modeⅡwill have no effect on DAC.  
PDM OUTPUTS 
The ADAU186x includes two channels of high performance, 1-
bit PDM outputs suitable for driving an external amplifier or 
other peripheral with low latency.  
Data can be routed to the PDM output path from the serial 
ports, the Tensilica core, the Fast DSP core, the ADCs, the 
digital microphones, the input ASRCs, the EQ, or the 
interpolator. 
These PDM outputs can operate with input sampling 
frequencies of 12 kHz, 24 kHz, 48 kHz, 96 kHz, 192 kHz, 384 
kHz, or 768 kHz. The sample rate is selectable via the PDM_FS 
bit. Ensure that all channels routed to the PDM outputs are at 
the same sample rate. The PDM output modulators can run 
either at 3.072 MHz or 6.144 MHz, which is selected via the 
PDM_RATE bit. This bit also determines the rate of the PDM 
output clock. The PDM output is sent over a 2-wire (PDM clock 
and PDM data) dual data rate interface. These two signals can 
be routed to any multipurpose (MPx) pin output via the 
respective MPx_MODE bits for each pin. 
PDM Outputs Full-Scale Level 
The full-scale PDM input results in the full-scale PDM outputs. 
The PDM modulator performance reduces at an output 
amplitude greater than −7.5 dBFS. 
PDM Outputs Volume Control and Filtering 
The volume of each PDM channel can be digitally attenuated 
using the PDMx_VOL registers. The volume can be set to be 
between +24 dB and −71.25 dB in 0.375 dB steps. The PDM 
volume can also be digitally muted in the PDMx_MUTE bits. 
By default, the volume control performs a soft ramp when 
changed, which can be bypassed for instantaneous change of 
volume via the PDM_HARD_VOL bit. The volume control for 
both channels can be set to use the Channel 0 volume via the 
PDM_VOL_LINK bit. When a PDM channel is enabled, it 
starts at the lowest volume setting and ramps, if 
PDM_HARD_VOL = 0, to the volume level set by its 
PDM_VOLx register. When a PDM channel is disabled, it 
ramps the volume from its current setting, if 
PDM_HARD_VOL = 0, to mute and then turn off. A high-pass 
filter is available on the PDM path to remove dc offsets. This 
filter can be enabled or disabled by using the PDMx_HPF_EN 
bits. The corner frequency of this high-pass filter is set to 1 Hz. 

=== PDF PAGE 24 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 24 of 337 
The order of the final interpolation filter can be selected via the 
PDM_MORE_FILT bit. Selecting the lower order filter results 
in lower propagation delay at the expense of lower attenuation 
of out of band components. 
ASYNCHRONOUS SAMPLE RATE CONVERTERS 
The ADAU186x includes ASRCs to enable asynchronous full- 
duplex operation of the serial ports. Four channels of ASRC are 
available for the digital outputs, and four channels of ASRC are 
available for the digital input signals.  
The ASRCs can convert serial output data from the internal rate 
of up to 192 kHz back down to 12 kHz. All intermediate 
frequencies and ratios are also supported.  
ASRCI can receive its inputs from one of the serial audio ports 
via the ASRCI_SOURCE bit. Each channel of the input ASRC 
can select its source from any of the 16 channels on the selected 
serial audio port via the ASRCIx_ROUTE bits. The output 
(internal) sample rate of the input ASRC is set via the 
ASRCI_OUT_FS bit.  
The output ASRC channels can receive their inputs from many 
internal sources via the ASRCOx_ROUTE bits: the Tensilica 
core, the Fast DSP core, the ADCs, the digital microphones, the 
EQ, or the decimator. Ensure that the sample rate of all sources 
to all of the channels of the output ASRC are at the same sample 
rate. The source of Channel 0 determines the internal sample 
rate of the output ASRC. The source of the channels to the 
output ASRC are set via the ASRCOx_ROUTE bits.  
The output sample rate of the output ASRC must be linked to 
one of the serial ports and can be selected via the 
ASRCO_SAI_SEL bit.  
The input and output ASRCs are fully independent and can 
operate on different serial ports.  
The ASRCs automatically mute their outputs to zero data when 
the outputs are not locked. The state of each ASRC lock can be 
monitored via the ASRCI_LOCK and ASRCO_LOCK read only 
status bits. In addition, unlocked to locked or locked to 
unlocked transitions of each ASRC can be used as an interrupt 
source to the four interrupt controllers.  
By default, the ASRCs use the high-performance mode of 
operation. A lower power, lower performance mode of 
operation can be enabled via each ASRCs ASRCx_LPM bit 
control.  
Additional filtering options are available to further customize 
the ASRCs to any application. Each ASRC has an 
ASRCx_VFILT bit that can enable a voice band filter that 
provides additional rejection at the Nyquist frequency, which 
can be useful when using traditional voice band sampling 
frequencies. There is also an ASRCx_MORE_FILT control bit 
for each ASRC that provides additionally filtering of out of band 
energy and may improve performance in some conditions. 
INTERPOLATION AND DECIMATION BLOCKS 
The ADAU186x includes blocks designed to convert audio 
from the fast sampling rate used for noise cancelling and the 
slow audio rate of the audio source. There are eight channels of 
fast to slow decimation and eight channels of slow to fast 
interpolation.  
Every two channel pairs of each block can independently 
operate at different input and output rates than the other two 
channel pairs. Ensure that the sampling rate of each two 
channel pair inputs matches when selecting the inputs via the 
routing register controls. The input sampling rates are 
determined by the FDECxx_IN_FS and FINTxx_IN_FS bits 
and the output sampling rates are determined by the 
FDECxx_OUT_FS and FINTxx_OUT_FS bits. For the 
interpolation block, the output rate must be set higher than the 
input rate. For the decimation block, the output rate must be 
set lower than the input rate. 
SIGNAL LEVELS 
Full-scale digital or 0 dBFS maps to the analog full-scale of the 
various converters. The FastDSP cores can maintain up to 24 
dBFS internally but clip symmetrically to 0 dBFS at their 
outputs. By default, there is no gain adjustment between any 
block. 
 
 

=== PDF PAGE 25 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 25 of 337 
EQUALIZER (EQ) 
The ADAU1850 integrates a MAC (Multiply Accumulate) 
Engine which supports following features:  
• Single cycle double precision MAC operation 
• Modulo address in Data RAM space 
• 3-stage pipeline 
The EQ input source includes:  any data register, any 
accumulator register, any serial port input channel, any ADC 
input, any ASRCI channel, any digital microphone input, FDSP 
output and Tensilica DSP output.  
The EQ band number is affected by FDSP_MODE through 
setting FDSP_MODE[0]. With default value 0, EQ works in 
Normal Mode and supports 13-band for sampling rate equal or 
below 192kHz. When value is set as 1, EQ works in Turbo Mode 
and supports 24-band for sampling rate equal or below 96kHz.  
POWER AND RUN CONTROL 
All program and parameter memory for the EQ can be read or 
written from any control port when POWER_EN = 1, and the 
PLL is locked, if used. The EQ starts processing when EQ_RUN 
= 1.  
EQ CLEAR 
When EQ isn’t running, the data ram can be cleared by writing 
1 to the EQ_CLEAR bit. The EQ_CLEAR_DONE bit in the 
EQ_STATUS register can be used to check if the EQ clear 
operation is done.   
READ/WRITE DATA FORMATS 
The read/write formats of the control port are byte oriented to 
allow ease of programming of common microcontrollers. To fit 
the data into a byte oriented format, 0s are added to the data 
fields before the MSB to extend the data-word to a full 8 bits. 
For example, 28-bit words written to the parameter RAM are 
preceded by four leading 0s to create a 32-bit (4-byte) word.  
MEMORY ADDRESS ALLOCATION 
When FDSP_MODE in Turbo Mode, the program memory and 
parameter memory address are shown in the Table 8. When 
FDSP_MODE in Normal Mode, program memory only 0x4000 
A000 ~0x4000 A1FF can be used. 
Table 9. Memory Address Allocation

PARAMETER BANK SWITCHING 
Two banks of parameter RAM are available: A and B. At any 
given time, the EQ only uses one of the two banks. The two 
banks allow parameters for filters and variables for other 
instructions to easily be switched between different processing 
scenarios. The bank used is selected with the EQ_BANK_SEL 
bit.  
PARAMETER MEMORY ACCESS 
Reads from any parameter bank from the I2C, SPI, UART are 
unrestricted if the EQ isn’t running. When EQ is running, only 
the unused parameter bank can be read from the I
2C, SPI, or 
UART port.  
Direct reads of in use banks from the I2C/SPI/UART are not 
allowed and return 0s. A read of the current bank returns all 0s. 
Writes to any parameter bank are possible when the EQ isn’t 
running. When EQ is running, only the unused parameter bank 
can be written from the I
2C, SPI, or UART port.

FASTDSP CORE 
The ADAU186x FastDSP core is optimized for ANC processing. 
The processing capabilities of the core include biquad filters, 
limiters, expanders, multipliers, bit wise operations, clippers, 
volume controls, and weighted mixing. The core has inputs 
from all sources and sixteen outputs. The core is controlled with 
a 27-bit program word, with a maximum of 96 instructions per 
frame. 
The FDSP instruction number is affected by FDSP_MODE 
through setting FDSP_MODE[0]. With default value 0, FDSP 
works in Normal Mode and supports maximum 64 instructions 
per frame for sampling rates equal or below 384kHz. When 
value is set as 1, FDSP works in turbo mode and supports 
maximum 96 instructions per frame per frame for sampling 
rates equal or below 192kHz.  
INSTRUCTIONS 
The instructions in the ADAU186x include the following:  
• Single precision (27-bit fractional precision) 
biquad/second-order filters 
• Double precision (54-bit fractional precision) 
biquad/second-order filters 
• Lower precision (19-bit fractional precision) 
biquad/second-order filters  
• Limiter with/without external detector loop or side chain 
input  
• Expander with/without external detector loop or side 
chain input  
• Volume slider  
• Two to four input scale and mix 
• Two input multiply  
• Absolute value   
• Symmetrical clipper  
• Min and Max 
• Shift  
• OR, AND, XOR, and INV  
• Memory read or write  
FILTER PRECISION 
Different levels of fractional precision are available for filters in 
the FastDSP core. Using lower fractional precision results in 
lower power consumption than using higher precision. 
However, care must be taken to ensure that filters have enough 
precision to maintain stability. 
FLAGS AND CONDITIONAL EXECUTION 
Several flags can be set or not set based on the output of each 
instruction. These flags include the following: 
• Output equals zero 
• Output is not equal to zero 
• Output is greater than zero 
• Output is less than zero 
• Output is greater than or equal to zero 
• Output is less than or equal to zero 
• Accumulator overflow 
Each instruction can always execute or conditionally execute 
based on an individual flag or other states. The other states are. 
• The logic state of MPx pins if used as GPIO input.  
• The FDSP_REG_COND0 to FDSP_REG_COND7 bits. 
• Modulo N Counter equals zero 
The GPIOs can be used on the MPx pins which are MP0-2, 
MP7-10, MP17, MP20, MP22-25, . The state of the MPx pins 
used as GPIOs determines whether or not an instruction 
executes.  
The FDSP_REG_CONDx bits are read/write bits that can be 
accessed via any of the control interfaces or via the Tensilica 
DSP. The state of these registers determines whether or not an 
instruction executes.  
The Modulo N counter is a counter that increments every 
frame of the FastDSP. The counter is reset to 0 after the 
number of frames is set in the FDSP_MOD_N bit. Instructions 
can execute every N frames set by the FDSP_MOD_N bit, 
which provides a mechanism to easily run some instructions at 
a lower rate than the frame rate.  
When an instruction does not execute based on a condition, the 
instruction can be set to either do nothing or pass its input to 
its output. 
INPUT SOURCES 
Any instruction can use any of the following as an input source: 
any data register, any accumulator register, any serial port 
input channel, any digital microphone input, any ADC input, 
any Tensilica DSP output, any ASRCI channel, any output 
from the interpolation block, or the EQ. 
The frame rate of the FastDSP must be set and determines 
when the program counter starts counting again at 0, which 
must be set to the sample rate of the fastest source. The source 
that the frame rate is determined by is set via the FDSP_RATE_ 
SOURCE bits. If desired, the frame rate can be set independent 
of any source, and the rate can be set via the FDSP_RATE_DIV 
bits. 
POWER AND RUN CONTROL 
All program, parameter, and data memories for the FastDSP 
can be read or written from any control interface or the 
Tensilica DSP when PWR_MODE[0]=1, FDSP_EN = 1, and 
the PLL is locked, if used.  
A single register FDSP_EN powers up the FastDSP core to 
allow access to the memories. The FastDSP core starts 
processing when both FDSP_EN = 1 and FDSP_RUN = 1.  

=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 27 of 337 
DATA MEMORY 
The ADAU186x FastDSP datapath is 28 bits (5.23 format) and 
up to 24 dBFS is allowed. All inputs and outputs to FastDSP are 
24 bits (1.23 format). The outputs are truncated to 24 bits so >0 
dBFS on an output results in clipping. The data memory is 96 
words. The double length memory enables the core to perform 
double precision arithmetic with double length data and single 
length coefficients.  
Each instruction has four associated data/state memory 
locations. These locations can read at any time via the control 
port interface or from the Tensilica DSP. 
PARAMETERS 
Parameters, such as filter coefficients, limiter settings, and 
volume control settings, are saved in parameter memories. Each 
parameter is a 32-bit number. The format of this number 
depends on the associated instruction. The number formats of 
the different parameters are shown in Table 9 for the biquad 
instructions.   
Table 10. Parameter Number Formats  
Parameter Type Format 
Filter Coefficient (B0, B1, B2,A1,A2) 5.27 
There are three parameter banks available. Each bank can hold 
a full set of 480 parameters (96 filters × 5 coefficients). Users 
can switch between Bank A, Bank B, and Bank C, allowing 
three sets of parameters to be saved in memory and switched 
on-the-fly while the core is running. Bank switching can be 
achieved by writing to the FDSP_BANK_SEL bits. Parameters 
in the active bank must only be updated via the FastDSP 
safeload registers while the core is running. If parameters are 
not updated in this way, a bad output may happen.  
Parameters are assigned to instructions in the order in which 
the instructions are instantiated in the code.  
PARAMETER BANK SWITCHING 
Three banks of parameters are available: A, B, and C. At any 
given time, the FastDSP uses only one of these banks. The three 
banks allow coefficients for filters and variables for other 
instructions to easily be switched between different processing 
scenarios. The bank used is selected with the 
FDSP_BANK_SEL bits.  
When the current bank is changed, the parameter values used 
for processing can either be changed on the next frame start or 
ramped via linear interpolation between the previously selected 
bank and the new bank indicated via the FDSP_BANK_SEL 
bits. To select this change or ramp, use the 
FDSP_RAMP_MODE bit. When the linear parameter ramp 
mode is selected, only the parameters associated with the three 
biquad instructions ramp. All parameters associated with other 
instructions change at the beginning of the next frame. 
Parameters in banks that are actively ramping do not change 
during a bank switch.  
It is possible to stop the linear ramp of parameters between the 
two values in the previous and current bank. The 
FDSP_LAMBDA bits are a 6-bit value representing the point 
along the linear interpolation curve between the two banks at 
which the bank ramp switch stops. The lambda value can be 
updated on-the-fly via the control interfaces but only increased 
after a ramped bank switch is initiated. To complete a bank 
switch, set a value of 63 (default setting). The actual current 
ramp point (0 to 63) can be read via the 
FDSP_CURRENT_LAMBDA bits. When this value reaches 63, 
the bank switch is complete, and the current parameters used 
match the current bank. Parameters in the two banks being 
ramped between cannot be modified while a ramped bank 
switch is occurring.  
An interrupt can be triggered to the interrupt controllers via 
the IRQx_PRAMP interrupt source bits. This interrupt triggers 
on the first frame when a ramped bank switch is active and 
FDSP_CURRENT_LAMBDA equals FDSP_LAMBDA.  
The rate at which the ramp between the two banks occurs is 
selectable via the FDSP_RAMP_RATE bits. 
PARAMETER BANK COPYING 
The parameters of any bank can be copied to any other bank 
with a single control write. There are six registers, 
FDSP_COPY_xy, for the six possible bank copy operations. 
Writing a 1 to one of these bits initiates a bank copy. After a 
bank copy initiates, the FastDSP waits until the start of the next 
frame, and then during the next frame copies the content of the 
banks while the associated instruction is executing. The bank 
copy completes at the start of the subsequent frame and takes at 
most two frames to complete from the initiation. Copying to 
the active bank is not permitted but results in no action being 
taken. 
PARAMETER MEMORY ACCESS 
Reads from any parameter memory bank from the I2C, SPI, 
UART or Tensilica DSP are unrestricted if the FastDSP core is 
enabled but not running. Reads of unused parameter banks 
from the I2C, SPI, UART or Tensilica DSP are unrestricted if 
the FastDSP core is enabled and running. While the core is 
running, if the control port (I2C/SPI/UART), or Tensilica DSP 
try to access the same location on the same cycle, the control 
port has priority, and the read from TDSP returns all 0s.  
Direct reads of in use banks from the I
2C/SPI/UART, mREAD 
instruction, or Tensilica DSP are not allowed and return 0s. A 
read of the current bank returns all 0s. Writes to all parameter 
banks are possible when the FastDSP core is enabled but not 
running. Writes to unused banks are possible at any time. 
While the core is running, if the control port (I2C/SPI/UART), 
or Tensilica DSP try to write to the same location on the same 

=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 28 of 337 
cycle, the control port has priority, and the write from the 
Tensilica DSP does not occur. 
The memory map from the perspective of the FDSP is different 
than the mapping of the memories to the control port or TDSP 
because the FDSP has its own address mapping, see the Table 
10.  When FDSP_MODE set as Turbo Mode, base address of 
instruction 0~95 can be used. When set as Normal Mode, only 
base address of instructions 0~63 can be used. 
FASTDSP PARAMETER SAFELOAD 
The parameter memory for a single instruction can be updated 
in real time on the active bank via the safeload mechanism over 
the control interface. Set the instruction number in the 
FDSP_SL_ADDR register, set the parameter values in the 
FDSP_SL_Py_x registers, and write a 1 to the 
FDSP_SL_UPDATE register. After these settings and write 
occur, all parameters for that instruction are updated at the 
same time with the values in the FDSP_SL_Py_x registers at the 
beginning of the next frame. Parameter safeload must be treated 
as an extra FastDSP instruction, and to enable using it, at least 
one instruction must be unused. 
There is a second FastDSP safeload interface that is mapped to 
the data memory space of the Tensilica DSP, which allows the 
Tensilica DSP to have word addressable access.

DIGITAL TURBO MODE 
In digital block, to provide more powerful digital function, 
Turb o Mode can be enabled through setting FDSP_MODE[0] 
to 1. Default mode is Normal Mode. All the register description 
in Register Summary and Register Details are about Normal 
Mode. Detailed differences between Normal Mode and Turbo 
Mode are shown as following. 
NORMAL MODE 
• Serial Audio Ports supporting I2S, Left justified, Right 
justified, or up to TDM16. 
• FDSP supports maximum 64 instructions per frame for 
sampling rates equal or below 384kHz. 
• Equalizer supports 13 - band for sampling rate equal or 
below 192kHz. 
TURBO MODE 
• Serial Audio Ports supporting I2S, Left justified, Right 
justified, or up to TDM12. In TDM mode, 
SPTx_OUT_ROUTE0 ~ SPTx_OUT_ROUTE11 can be 
used normally. SPTx_OUT_ROUTE15 will output a 
mixing of SAI0_0 and SAI0_1 (SAI0_0_high 16 bits << 16 
& SAI0_1_high 8 bits).  
• FDSP supports maximum 96 instructions per frame for 
sampling rates equal or below 192kHz. Detailed memory 
information please refer to FastDSP Core section.  
• Equalizer supports 24 - band for sampling rate equal or 
below 96kHz. Detailed memory information please refer to 
Equalizer section.

TENSILICA DSP 
The Tensilica HiFi-3z DSP (TDSP) features a 32-bit, audio DSP 
engine optimized for audio and voice processing and other 
demanding DSP functions. The Tensilica HiFi-3z core combines 
quad 24-bit x 24-bit fixed point MACs or two 32-bit x 32-bit 
fixed point MACs with 64-bit accumulators. The processor 
provides on-chip debug support with 
control of the software state of the processor through the JTAG 
port. 
DMA 
A DMA was provided to transfer data between memory to 
memory, memory to peripheral, peripheral to memory. The 
DMA has the lowest priority to access the memory compared to 
the control port and TDSP core. 
FIFO 
FIFO block is provided to access the SRAM. 
DATA SYNC 
Data Sync block is used to transmit and receive data between 
TDSP and audio peripherals.  
UART 
The ADAU186x has a standard UART serial interface that 
provides a simple mechanism to communicate with other serial 
devices.  
QSPI 
One dedicated QSPI interface intended for external flash 
memory connection, which features memory mapped registers 
with support for direct read of SPI flash memory from the 
TDSP core. Using the QSPI interface for program and data 
loading if self-boot mode is enabled. The QSPI interface can be 
configured through the QSPI registers during the boot up, 
details shown in the register map. And it supports the 
maximum clock up to 50MHz. 
WATCHDOG 
A watchdog monitors the Tensilica DSP execution. Once the 
watchdog timeout, it will generate interrupt or reset the 
Tensilica DSP .  Watchdog timer can be programmed through 
the registers. 
GENERAL-PURPOSE TIMER 
The ADAU186x integrates a low frequency timer which 
running at 32.768kHz. This timer can be used to generate 
interrupt and provide periodic events. 
INTERRUPT 
There are fifteen level trigger interrupt sources coming from 
FFSRAM0, FFSRM1, FFSRAM2, QSPI, Data Sync, DMA, 
UART, SPI, four AON_IRQx and three MPI_IRQx. 
DEBUG INTERFACE 
The ADAU186x provides a JTAG interface for the debugging. 
The Figure 10 shows the connection between the ADAU186x 
and the JTAG. And make sure that the voltage level from the 
JTAG is 1.8V since the ADAU186x uses 1.8V as the IO voltage.

MEMORY MAPPING  
The memory of the ADAU186x is shown in Figure 11.  
An internal 4kB read only memory (ROM) contains boot code 
and the boot ROM executes at system reset. 
The L1 memory system is the highest performance memory 
available to the TDSP core and includes 32kB L1 instruction 
RAM, 16kB L1 data RAM0 and 16kB L1 data RAM1. The TDSP 
core also contains 24kB instruction cache and 24kB data cache. 
224kB L2 memory is available which includes: 64kB System 
RAM, 64kB FFSRAM0, 64kB FFSRAM1 and 32kB FFSRAM2. 
The ADAU186x memory address space supports up to 16MB of 
external serial quad flash memory connected to the QSPI port.  
The TDSP core reads from flash memory are directly cached via 
internal cache, and direct memory mapped reads are permitted. 
The QSPI flash port is used for self-boot mode. 
All the control and configuration registers are mapped into 
memory mapped registers through a 32 bit address which 
includes DMA registers, QSPI registers, SoC registers, SPI 
registers, UART registers, Timer registers, Watchdog registers, 
always on (AON) registers, FFSRAM0 registers, FFSRAM1 
registers, FFSRAM2 registers, and DATA_SYNC registers. 
5kB memory is allocated to FDSP , 1.5kB memory is allocated to 
EQ and 768B memory is allocated to Data sync function block. 
Memories can be accessed by the TDSP core internally or 
externally from the control port, I2C/SPI/UART.

POWER SAVING OPTIONS  
The ADAU186x offers multiple options to balance the 
performance and power in some of the blocks.  
ADC BIAS CURRENT CONTROL 
The ADCs provide a mechanism to modify the each channel’s 
bias current level independently via the ADCx_IBIAS and 
ADCx_IBIAS control bits. The ADC performance is very 
similar in different bias current level, but the power 
consumption is a little different. Please refer to the Table 11. 
Playback Path Working Mode Control 
The Playback path (DAC and headphone driver) provides a 
mechanism to tradeoff between performance and power 
consumption options for the user. Two possible settings can be 
set via the PB_POWER_CTRL control bit. Please refer to the 
Table 12. 
PLL Bypass 
Bypassing the PLL saves power. Please refer to the Table 13.

CONTROL PORT 
The ADAU186x has a 4-wire SPI control port, a 2-wire I2C 
control port and an UART control port. These three types 
control port share four pins. Each port can set the memories 
and registers.  
The IC defaults to I2C mode but can be put into SPI control 
mode or UART control mode. By pulling the SS pin low three 
times, in the meanwhile, toggling the SCLK pin three times, the 
IC can enter SPI mode. By toggling the UART RX pin 3 times, 
in the meanwhile, keeping the SCLK pin in low or high level, 
the IC can enter UART mode. The detailed methods to switch the 
control mode from I2C to SPI or UART are listed in Table 17. 
The control port is capable of full read/write operation for all 
addressable memories and registers. Most signal processing 
parameters are controlled by writing new values to the 
parameter memories using the control port. Other functions, 
such as mute and input/output mode control, are programmed 
through the registers.  
All addresses can be accessed in either single address mode or 
burst mode. The first byte (Byte 0) of a control port write 
contains the 7-bit IC address plus R/
W bit. The next four bytes 
(Byte 1 to Byte 4) are the 32-bit subaddress of the memory or 
register location within the ADAU186x. All subsequent bytes 
(starting with Byte 5) contain the data, such as the register, 
program, or parameter data. The exact formats for specific 
types of writing are shown in Figure 14 and Figure 15. 
If large blocks of data must be downloaded to the ADAU186x 
FastDSP core or Tensilica DSP , the output of the core can be 
disabled, new data can be loaded, and the core can then be 
restarted. This restart is typically done during the booting 
sequence at start-up or when loading a new program into 
memory. The control port pins are multifunctional, depending 
on the mode in which the device is operating.  Table 18 
describes these multiple functions. 
BURST MODE COMMUNICATION 
Burst mode addressing, in which the sub-addresses are 
automatically incremented at word boundaries, can be used for 
writing large amounts of data to contiguous memory locations. 
This increment happens automatically after a single-word write 
unless the control port communication is stopped (that is, a stop 
condition is issued for I
2C, or SS is brought high for SPI, or a 
tail byte is issued for UART). The registers and RAMs in the 
ADAU186x are 4 bytes width, except the AON registers which 
is 1 byte width, so the auto-increment feature knows the mapping 
between subaddresses and the word length of the destination 
register (or memory location).

READING AND WRITING TO MEMORIES 
All Tensilica DSP and FastDSP memory locations are larger 
than a single byte. While each byte occupies a single address 
when communicating over a control interface (I
2C or SPI or 
UART), when writing to these memories, an entire memory 
word must be written starting with the lowest address and 
continuing sequentially to the highest address for a write to 
actually occur. Similarly, a read must begin at the lowest 
memory address. However, for reads, all locations must be read. 
The mapping of bytes over the control interface is the least 
significant byte, or a memory location is written or read first, 
and the most significant byte is written or read last. The 
memories can be read or written in burst mode or single byte 
mode so that the proceeding requirements are met. 
Table 21. Example write to Tensilica DSP Program RAM 
Word 0 
Address Data 
0x 5FFF 8000 data[7:0] 
0x 5FFF 8001 data[15:8] 
0x 5FFF 8002 data[23:16] 
0x 5FFF 8003 data[31:24] (the memory actually gets written 
after this write) 
I2C CONTROL PORT 
The ADAU186x supports a 2-wire serial (I2C-compatible) 
microprocessor bus driving multiple peripherals. I2C uses two 
pins, serial data (SDA) and serial clock (SCL), to carry data 
between the ADAU186x and the system I2C master controller. 
In I2C mode, the ADAU186x is always a slave on the bus.  
The device supports fast mode plus I2C operation, but for most 
bus capacitances, the SDA_MISO_DRIVE bit must be set to 1 
to support these operating speeds.  
Each slave device is recognized by a unique 7-bit device 
address. The ADAU186x I2C address format is shown in Table 
21. The LSB of this first byte sent from the I2C master sets 
either a read or write operation. Logic Level 1 corresponds to a 
read operation, and Logic Level 0 corresponds to a write 
operation.  
Pin ADDR0 and Pin ADDR1 set the LSBs of the I
2C address 
(see Table 21). Therefore, each ADAU186x can be set to one of 
four unique addresses, allowing multiple ICs to exist on the 
same I
2C bus without address contention. The 7-bit I2C 
addresses are shown in Table 22. 
An I2C data transfer is always terminated by a stop condition. 
Both SDA and SCL must have 2.0 kΩ pull -up resistors on the 
lines connected to these pins. The voltage on these signal lines 
cannot be higher than IOVDD. 
Table 22. I2C Address Format  
Bit 6 Bit 5 Bit 4 Bit 3 Bit 2 Bit 1 Bit 0  
1 1 0 0 1 ADDR1 ADDR0 
Table 23. I2C Addresses  
ADDR1 ADDR0 Slave Address 
0 0 0x64 
0 1 0x65 
1 0 0x66 
1 1 0x67 
Addressing 
Initially, each device on the IP2PC bus is in an idle state and 
monitoring the SDA and SCL lines for a start condition and the 
proper address. The IP2PC master initiates a data transfer by 
establishing a start condition, defined by a high to low 
transition on SDA while SCL remains high, indicating that an 
address/data stream follows. All devices on the bus respond to 
the start condition and shift the next eight bits (the 7-bit 
address plus the R/
W bit) MSB first. The device that recognizes 
the transmitted address responds by pulling the data line low 
during the ninth clock pulse. This ninth bit is known as an 
acknowledge bit. All other devices withdraw from the bus at 
this point and return to the idle condition. The R/
W bit 
determines the direction of the data. A Logic 0 on the LSB of 
the first byte indicates that the master writes information to the 
peripheral, whereas a Logic 1 indicates that the master reads 
information from the peripheral after writing the subaddress 
and repeating the start address. A data transfer takes place until 
a stop condition is encountered. A stop condition occurs when 
SDA transitions from low to high while SCL is held high. 
Figure 
12 shows the timing of an I2C write and Figure 13 shows an I2C 
read. 
Stop and start conditions can be detected at any stage during 
the data transfer. If these conditions are asserted out of 
sequence with normal read and write operations, the 
ADAU186x immediately jumps to the idle condition. During a 
given SCL high period, the user can only issue one start 
condition, one stop condition, or a single stop condition 
followed by a single start condition. A no-acknowledge 
condition is where the SDA line is not pulled low on the ninth 
clock pulse on SCL. If an invalid subaddress is issued by the 
user, the ADAU186x issues an acknowledge, but no data write 
occurs, and a read returns zero. If the highest subaddress 
location is reached during in write mode, the data for the 
invalid byte is not loaded to any subaddress register.

I2C Read and Write Operations 
Figure 14 shows the timing of a single word write operation. 
Every ninth clock pulse, the ADAU186x issues an acknowledge 
by pulling SDA low.  
Figure 15 shows the timing of a burst mode write sequence 
where the target destination words are four bytes. The 
ADAU186x knows to increment its subaddress register every 
four bytes because the requested subaddress corresponds to a 
register or memory area with a 4-byte word length.  
The timing of a single word read operation is shown in 
Figure 16. Note that the first R/
W bit is 0, indicating a write 
operation because the subaddress still must be written to set up 
the internal address. After the ADAU186x acknowledges the 
receipt of the subaddress, the master must issue a repeated start 
command, followed by the chip address byte with R/W set to 1 
(read), causing the ADAU186x SDA to reverse and begin 
driving data back to the master. The master then responds 
every ninth pulse with an acknowledge pulse to the 
ADAU186x.  
Figure 17 shows the timing of a burst mode read sequence 
where the target read words are four bytes. The ADAU186x 
increments its subaddress every four bytes because the 
requested subaddress corresponds to a register or memory area 
with word lengths of four bytes. The ADAU186x always

bytes.

SPI CONTROL PORT 
By default, the ADAU186x is in I2C mode, but the device can 
be put in SPI control mode by pulling SS low three times by 
issuing three SPI writes, which are in turn ignored by the 
ADAU186x. Recommend reading the VENDOR_ID register 
(address 0x 4000 C000) three times with SPI command from 
the external processor to enter to SPI mode. The next (fourth) 
SPI write is then latched in the SPI port.  
The SPI port uses a 4-wire interface, consisting of 
SS, SCLK, 
MOSI, and MISO signals, and is always a slave port. The SS  
signal must go low at the beginning of a transaction and high at 
the end of a transaction. The SCLK signal latches MOSI on a 
low to high transition. MISO data is shifted out of the 
ADAU186x on the falling edge of SCLK and must be clocked to 
a receiving device, such as a microcontroller, on the SCLK 
rising edge. The MOSI signal carries the serial input data, and 
the MISO signal is the serial output data. The MISO signal 
remains tristated until a read operation is requested, allowing 
other SPI-compatible peripherals to share the same readback 
line. The SCLK is low when clock is inactive. 
All SPI transactions have the same basic format shown in Table 
23. The timing diagrams for SPI write and SPI read are shown 
in Figure 18 and Figure 19, respectively. All data must be 
written MSB first. The ADAU186x can only be taken out of SPI 
mode by pulling the PD pin low or by powering down the IC. 
Read/Write 
The first byte of an SPI transaction indicates whether the com-
munication is a read or a write with the R/W bit. The LSB of this 
first byte determines whether the SPI transaction is a read (Logic 
Level 1) or a write (Logic Level 0). 
Subaddress 
The 32-bit subaddress word is decoded into a location in one of 
the memories or registers. This subaddress is the location of the 
appropriate memory location or register.  
It is necessary to add an unused byte of zeros after the sub-address.   
Data Bytes 
The number of data bytes varies according to the register or 
memory being accessed. During a burst mode write, an initial 
subaddress is written followed by a continuous sequence of data 
for consecutive memory/register locations. 
A sample timing diagram for a single-write SPI operation to the 
parameter RAM is shown in Figure 18. A sample timing diagram 
of a single-read SPI operation is shown in Figure 19. The MISO 
pin goes from tristate to being driven at the beginning of Byte 5. 
In this example, Byte 0 to Byte 4 contain the addresses and the 
R/
W bit and subsequent bytes carry the data.
