UART CONTROL PORT 
By default, the ADAU186x is in I2C mode, but the device can be 
put in UART mode by receiving a specific data which has three 
times of rising edge on the UART_CTRL_RX pin of the 
ADAU186x. Recommend reading the VENDOR_ID register 
(address 0x 4000 C000) with UART read command (ID must be 
0x 00) from the external processor to enter to UART mode.  
The UART port uses two pins, UART_CTRL_TX and 
UART_CTRL_RX, to transmit data and receive data. 
ADAU186x UART works in half duplex mode and supports 
auto baud rate from 1200 to 1M. Each data-word contains a 
start bit, eight data bits, a bit for even parity and one stop bit. 
When using UART as the control port, it is necessary that an 
input clock on the MCLKIN pin is ready or the external crystal 
is provided before the first UART communication. It is 
recommended to use a lower baud rate at initial stage due to the 
limitation of the clock on the MCLKIN which is perhaps lower 
as 30kHz. After the PLL configuration by the UART port, 
higher baud rate can be used depending on the clock setting.  
Write Frame 
When a microprocessor sends a write frame to ADAU186x by 
UART control port, the frame must comply with the format 
which is shown in Figure 20. It includes one byte of Head, one 
byte of ID, one byte of write command, four bytes of the start 
address of writing data, two bytes of the data length which will 
be wrote to ADAU186x, N bytes of data, one byte of CRC, and 
one byte of Tail. After ADAU186x receives the byte of Tail, 
ADAU186x will send back a frame on the UART_CTRL_TX 
line following the byte of Tail on the UART_CTRL_RX line, 
starting with one byte of head and ending with one byte of Tail, 
includes one byte of ID, one byte of Status, and one byte of 
CRC. 
Read Frame 
When a microprocessor sends a read frame to ADAU186x by 
UART control port, the frame must comply with the format 
which is shown in Figure 21. It includes one byte of Head, one 
byte of ID, one byte of read command, four bytes of the start 
address of reading data, two bytes of the data length which will 
be read from ADAU186x, one byte of CRC, and one byte of Tail. 
After ADAU186x receives the byte of Tail, ADAU186x will send 
back a frame on the UART_CTRL_TX line following the byte of 
Tail on the UART_CTRL_RX line, starting with one byte of 
head and ending with one byte of Tail, includes one byte of ID, 
one byte of Status, N bytes of data read from ADAU186x, and 
one byte of CRC. 
Head and Tail 
When a microprocessor sends a frame to ADAU186x, each 
transaction must start with a byte of head (0x7F) and end with a 
byte of tail (0xF0) no matter a write or a read frame.  
When ADAU186x sends back a frame, it will also comply with 
the format that start with a byte of head and end with a byte of 
tail.  
ID 
The number of the ID can be an arbitrary number and diverse 
in different frame. But the ADAU186x will send back the ID 
which is same with the ID that ADAU186x received from the 
microprocessor in one frame.  
Read/Write 
The third byte of an UART transaction frame indicates whether 
the communication is a read or a write with the R/W bit. The 
MSB of this third byte determines whether the UART 
transaction is a read (Logic Level 1) or a write (Logic Level 0). 
CRC 
An 8-bit CRC validates the content of the UART frame. This 
CRC is strong enough to detect single error bursts of up to eight 
bits in size.  The polynomial for the CRC is x8+x7+x3+x2+1 and 
the CRC seed is 0x FF . 
Each write frame or read frame contains CRC on both 
UART_CTRL_RX line generated by the microprocessor and 
UART_CTRL_TX line generated by the ADAU186x.  For a 
write frame, the one byte CRC generated by the microprocessor 
covers the content from the ID to the last number of Data. The 
one byte CRC generated by the ADAU186x covers the content 
of ID and Status (See Figure 20). For a read frame, the one byte 
CRC generated by the microprocessor covers the content from 
the ID to the byte of LENL. The one byte CRC generated by the 
ADAU186x covers the content from the ID to the last number 
of Data sent to microprocessor (See Figure 21). 
If customer doesn’t need the CRC feature, customer can write 
any data in the byte of CRC sent from microprocessor and 
ignore the byte of CRC and the CRC flag in the byte of Status 
sent from ADAU186x. 
Status Information 
When ADAU186x sends information to the microprocessor in a 
write frame or a read frame, a byte of status will be contained. 
The higher 4 bits show the previous transaction status which 
includes CRC, integrity and parity. Similar to the lower 4 bits 
which show the current transaction status (See Figure 22 for 
details).  
If there is CRC error during a write frame or a read frame, the 
CRC bit in the byte of Status will be set to 1. Otherwise it will be 
0. 
If there is integrity error during a write frame or a read frame, 
the integrity bit in the byte of Status will be set to 1. Otherwise 
it will be 0. 

=== PDF PAGE 40 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 40 of 337 
If there is parity error during a write frame or a read frame, the 
parity bit in the byte of Status will be set to 1. Otherwise it will 
be 0. 
Recover from an Abnormal Frame 
If any abnormity happens when the microprocess writes a write 
or read frame to ADAU186x, the microprocess can send one 
byte of Head and then follow another byte of Head to start a 
new transaction.  
Figure 23 shows an abnormal case, once the ADAU186x 
recognizes the second Head, the previous transaction will be 
discarded immediately no matter response or not yet. The event 
will be recorded, the status information will be sent in the 
higher 4 bits of the status data. 
ESCAPE 
During the write or read frame, escape is supported and 
performed during the UART frame between Head and Tail if 
the data is same with the Head or Tail. The escape formats are 
shown in below. 
Escape Head:  0x7F = 0xFF + 0x5F 
Escape Tail: 0xF0 = 0xFF + 0xD0 
Escape None: 0xFF = 0xFF + 0xDF

Figure 21. UART Read Frame

SELF BOOT 
There are two boot mode in the ADAU186x: self-boot mode, or 
non-self-boot mode.  
The ADAU186x works in self-boot mode when the SELFBOOT 
pin is set high at power up and PD, otherwise it works in non-
self-boot mode. The state of the SELFBOOT pin is checked 
internally only when the ADAU186x comes out of a reset via 
the PD pin going high or by applying power with PD already set 
high. When the device comes out of reset and there is a MCLK 
source present (on the MCLKIN pin), the state of the 
SELFBOOT pin is registered to determine whether to self-boot. 
At reset, the state is set not to self-boot. Therefore, if there is no 
clock at the MCLKIN pin, the self-boot does not occur.  
During booting, ensure that there is a stable DVDD in the 
system. The 
PD pin must remain high during self-boot 
operation. If the SELFBOOT pin is not used for a multipurpose 
pin function, tie the pin to either IOVDD or DGND.   
In the self-boot mode, the TDSP actively load program and data 
through the QSPI. In the self-boot mode, ensure that the clock 
on the MCLK pin is higher than 11MHz. The 
ROM_BOOT_MODE pin determines the ROM boot up mode. 
when ROM_BOOT_MODE pin is high, the internal QSPI 
controller will using the un-packed mode to load program and 
data from external flash memory. When ROM_BOOT_MODE 
pin is low, the internal QSPI controller will using the packed 
mode to load program and data from external flash memory. 
In the non-self-boot mode, the processors in the ADAU186x 
receives program and data from external host devices through 
control port, I
2C/SPI/UART.  
 
 

=== PDF PAGE 42 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 42 of 337 
MULTIPURPOSE PINS 
The ADAU186x has twenty-six multipurpose (MPx) pins that 
can be used for general purpose input, general purpose output, 
clock outputs, interrupts out, interrupts input and PDM 
outputs. Each pin can be individually set to either its default or 
MPx setting. The function of each of these pins is set in using 
the MPx_MODE bits. By default, each pin is configured as its 
normal function. Care must be taken when using SELFBOOT/ 
MP19 or ROM_BOOT_MODE/MP21 as multipurpose pins. 
The state of these two pins at power-up (later of either 
PD pin 
going high or power being applied with the PD pin already 
high) determine whether the device self boots and ROM boot 
up mode, which must still be followed even if the pin is used for 
another multipurpose function later.  
When a MPx pin is set as a general-purpose input, the MPx pin 
can be read via all control interfaces via the GPIOx_IN bits, the 
pin can also be read and acted upon by the Tensilica DSP core, 
and the pin can be used to conditionally execute instructions or 
trigger the compressor in the FastDSP .  
When an MPx pin is set as general-purpose output, the state of the 
pin can be set via all control interfaces using the GPIOx_OUT bits 
or by the Tensilica DSP core.   
Any MPx pin can be used as a master clock output. The rate of 
the master clock output is determined by the MCLKO_RATE 
bits. Multiple pins can be used as this function if desired.  
Any MPx pin can be used to output the PDM clock or data 
signal for the PDM output interface. Any MPx pin can be used 
to output the interrupt status from the four interrupt sources. 
Any MPx pin can be used as the interrupt input and the event 
will be record in the MP_INPUT_IRQx_STATUSx registers. 
Table 25. Multipurpose Pin Functions  
MPx Pin Function Direction 
Normal Operation Default 
General Purpose Input (GPI) In 
General Purpose Output  from GPIOx_OUT 
register (GPO_REG) 
Out 
General Purpose Output  from Tensilica DSP 
(GPO_TDSP) 
Out 
MCLK Output (MCLKO) Out 
IRQ1 Output (IRQ1) Out 
IRQ2 Output (IRQ2) Out 
IRQ3 Output (IRQ3) Out 
IRQ4 Output (IRQ4) Out 
IRQ Input In 
PDM Clock Output Out 
PDM Data Output Out

DMIC67 In 
MPx Pin Function Direction 
DMIC_CLK0_INV Out 
DMIC_CLK1 Out 
Interrupts 
Each multipurpose pin can be used to output one of four 
interrupts that have various sources when selected for this 
function. The sources of the interrupts are for the AVDD 
undervoltage warning, PLL locked or unlocked, DAC and ADC 
channels clipping, input and output ASRCs locked or unlocked, 
EQ memory clear done, FDSP output not zero, parameter ramp 
up complete, DLDO DVS done, power up complete, serial port 
locked or unlocked, phase sync locked or unlocked and the 
generic Tensilica DSP interrupts. Each interrupt source can be 
individually masked with their respective IRQx_MASKx 
registers. Each interrupt output can be set to active low or active 
high output on the pin selected for the interrupt output via the 
IRQx_PINOUT_POL bits. The status of each interrupt source 
can be read via the IRQ status registers (IRQx_STATUSx). 
When an interrupt source is masked, if that interrupt becomes 
true, the interrupt is shown in the interrupt status registers but 
does not cause the MPx pin (if set as interrupt output) to show 
an interrupt. All sources of each interrupt are cleared via a write 
of 1 to the IRQx_CLEAR bits. The interrupt status bits are 
sticky, such that if an interrupt source becomes true, the status 
reads 1 until a clear occurs, even if that interrupt source is no 
longer true. The Tensilica DSP interrupts are initiated by the 
Tensilica DSP writing the SW_INTx bits. 
The multipurpose pin can be used as interrupt input and the 
event will be record in the MP_INPUT_IRQx_STATUSx 
register which will trig an interrupt to the Tensilica. 
Each interrupt input source can be individually masked with 
their respective MP_INPUT_IRQx_MASKx registers.   
Each interrupt input can be set to active low, active high, rising 
edge or falling edge on the pin selected for the interrupt input 
via the MP_INPUT_IRQx_MODE bits. 
The MP_INPUT_IRQx_STATUS value will be cleaned via a 
write of 1 to the MP_INPUT_IRQx_CLEAR bit. 
Pin Controls 
Each pin that can be used as a multipurpose pin has several 
control selections to set various setting. When the pin is used as 
an output, the drive strength can be selected at 2 mA, 4 mA, 8 
mA, or 12 mA. In addition, a weak pull-up or pull-down can be 
selected. These settings are in their respective pin control 
register. These pin control settings affect the pins operation in 
both normal functional mode and when used in all 
multipurpose pin modes. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 43 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 43 of 337 
SERIAL DATA PORTS 
The serial data input and output ports of the ADAU186x can be 
set to accept or transmit data in a 2-channel format such as I2S 
or up to 12 channels in a time division multiplexing (TDM) 
stream to interface to external ADCs, DACs, DSPs, and system 
on chips (SOCs). Data is processed in twos complement, MSB 
first format. The left channel data field always precedes the 
right channel data field in 2-channel streams.  
The serial data clocks do not need to be synchronous with the 
ADAU186x master clock input, but the frame clock and bit 
clock must be synchronous to each other. The FSYNC_x and 
BCLK_x pins are used to clock both the serial input and output 
ports. The FSYNC_x and BCLK_x pins can also be used as a 
source to the PLL to provide the main chip clock. Each serial 
port can be set to be either the master or the slave in a system. 
Because there is only one set of serial data clocks, the input and 
output of a single port must always both be either master or slave.  
The SPTx_SAI_MODE bits set whether the serial port is 
operating in stereo mode or TDM mode. In stereo modes, both 
edges of frame clock determine where data is placed, and the 
left channel maps to the output for Channel 0, while the right 
channel maps to the output for Channel 1. In TDM mode only, 
the rising edge of frame clock determine where data is placed. 
In TDM mode, each channel of data receives a slot that can be 
either 16, 24, or 32 BCLKs wide. The width of each slot is 
determined by the SPTx_SLOT_WIDTH bits. In TDM mode, 
the slot number is affected by register FDSP_MODE[0] value. 
Detailed information please refer to the Digital Turbo Mode 
section. 
The serial data control registers allow control of the clock 
polarity and the data input modes. The valid data formats are 
I2S (delay by 1), left justified (delay by 0), or right justified 
(delay by 8, 12, or 16 BCLKs). The delay indicates the number 
of bit clocks BCLKs from the rising/falling edge of frame clock 
FSYNC_x where the MSB of the data is placed in stereo modes, 
and the number of bit clocks BCLKs from the rising of frame 
clock in TDM mode. In all modes except for the right justified 
mode, the serial port inputs an arbitrary number of bits up to a 
limit of 24. Extra bits do not cause an error, but the bits are 
ignored. The serial port can operate with an arbitrary number 
of bit clock BCLK_x transitions in each frame clock frame. 
When using a high bit clock rate (12.288 MHz or higher), it is 
recommended to increase the drive strength settings for the 
output signal pins. The high drive strength effectively speeds up 
the transition times of the waveforms, thereby improving the 
signal integrity of the clock and data lines. The drive strength 
can be set in the pad drive strength registers. 
Table 25 describes the proper serial port settings for standard 
audio data formats. More information about the settings in 
Table 25 can be found in the SPTx_CTRLx register descriptions.  
The polarity of both frame clock and bit clock can be inverted 
via the SPTx_LRCLK_POL and SPTx_BCLK_POL bits. These 
bits do not need to be used to support the typical formats 
shown in Table 25. Setting either SPTx_LRCLK_POL or 
SPTx_BCLK_POL to 1 places an inverter at the input to the 
serial port on its respective signal. For example, while serial 
data and frame clock are normally sampled on the rising edge of 
bit clock, setting SPTx_BCLK_POL = 1 samples on the falling 
edge of bit clock.  
Each serial port can be set to be a master, in which case BCLK_x 
and FSYNC_x are driven as outputs. The output rate and 
direction of these two signals are set via the SPTx_LRCLK_SRC 
and SPTx_BCLK_SRC bits. A bit clock rate higher than 24.576 
MHz cannot be generated. Therefore, the settings of these 
registers that request this rate result in no bit clock.  
Unused bit slots can be tristated so that multiple ICs can drive a 
single serial data bus, which is controlled via the SPTx_TRI_ 
STATE bit. For example, in a 32-bit TDM frame with 24-bit 
data, the eight unused bits are tristated. Inactive channels are 
also tristated for one full frame each. Serial output channels are 
disabled when the SPTx_OUT_ROUTEy bits are set to 0x3F . 
Note that the timing for serial data output changes based on the 
minimum IOVDD voltage. While the serial ports can work for 
inputting a signal on SDATAI_x for any IOVDD and bit clock 
rate within the specification, the delay on SDATAO_x at 1.1 V 
excludes operating at higher bit clock rates.

APPLICATIONS INFORMATION 
POWER SUPPLY BYPASS CAPACITORS 
Each analog and digital power supply pin should be bypassed to 
its nearest appropriate ground pin with a single 0.1 μF capacitor. 
The connections to each side of the capacitor should be as short 
as possible, and the trace should be routed on a single layer with no 
vias. For maximum effectiveness, locate the capacitor equidistant 
from the power and ground pins or slightly closer to the power pin 
if equidistant placement is not possible. Thermal connections to 
the ground planes should be made on the far side of the capacitor.  
Each supply signal on the board should also be bypassed with a 
single bulk capacitor (10 μF to 47 μF). 
 
Figure 26. Recommended Power Supply Bypass Capacitor Layout 
LAYOUT 
The HPVDD, HPVDD_L supply is for the headphone 
amplifiers. If the headphone amplifiers are enabled, the PCB trace 
to this pin should be wider than traces to other pins to increase the 
current carrying capacity. A wider trace should also be used for 
the headphone output lines. 
GROUNDING 
A single ground plane should be used in the application layout. 
Components in an analog signal path should be placed away 
from digital signals.

DMA CHANNEL PRIMARY CONTROL DATA BASE POINTER REGISTER 
Address: 0x40000008, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: DMA_PRI_BASE_PTR 
The DMA_DMA_PRI_BASE_PTR register must be programmed to point to the primary channel control base pointer in the system 
memory. The amount of system memory that must be assigned to the DMA controller depends on the number of DMA channels used 
and whether the alternate channel control data structure is used. This register cannot be read when the DMA controller is in the reset 
state. 
Table 31. Bit Descriptions for DMA_PRI_BASE_PTR

0x0 R/W 
 
DMA CHANNEL ALTERNATE CONTROL DATA BASE POINTER REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000000C, Reset: 0x00000200, Name: DMA_ALT_BASE_PTR 
The DMA_DMA_ALT_BASE_PTR read-only register returns the base address of the alternate channel control data structure. This 
register removes the necessity for application software to calculate the base address of the alternate data structure. This register cannot be 
read when the DMA controller is in the reset state.

error status is set if the controller encountered a bus error while 
performing a transfer. If a bus error occurs on a channel, that 
channel is automatically disabled by the controller. The other 
channels are unaffected. 
The index value will be reset when the user clear the interrupt 
status by writting DMA_INT_STATUS_CLR[31]. 
 When Read: 
 0 – No bus error occurred. 
1 – A bus error control is pending. 
0x0 R/W1C 
 
DMA PER CHANNEL INVALID DESCRIPTOR CLEAR REGISTER

[19:0] DMA_INVDESC_IDX_CLR  DMA Invalid Descriptor Index. DMA Invalid Descriptor Index 
This register is used to read the per channel DMA invalid 
descriptor status. The per channel  invalid descriptor status is set if 
the controller reads an invalid descriptor (whose cycle control is 
3'b000). If it reads invalid cycle control for a channel, that channel 
is automatically disabled by the controller. The other channels are 
unaffected.  
The index value will be reset when the user clear the interrupt 
status by writting DMA_INT_STATUS_CLR[30]. 
 When Read: 
0 – No invalid cycle control occurred. 
1 – An invalid cycle control is pending.

REGISTER DETAILS: SERIAL PERIPHERAL INTERFACE 
 CONTROL REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001004, Reset: 0x00000040, Name: QSPI_CTRL 
The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL register enables the QSPI and configures settings for operating modes, communication protocols, and buffer 
operations. 
Table 55. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_CTRL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
31 QSPI_MEMMAP_EN  Memory-Mapped SPI Enable. When the 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MEMMAP_EN bit is asserted, 
communication to a SPI memory device is automated such 
that the memory it contains is accessible directly through 
the read of processor address space assigned to it (As far 
as the SPI peripheral is concerned, this includes all read 
accesses received by the SPI peripheral’s system crossbar 
slave port). Note that when memory-mapped access of SPI 
memory is enabled, attempts to communicate with the SPI 
device using legacy methods are blocked and receive 
fabric error responses are generated. Legacy methods 
include any direct access made to the TX and Rx FIFOs, 
whether initiated by DMA or processor MMR access. 
Blocked attempts also set the ?.:QSPI_STATUS:MMAE? bit 
notification bit. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Hardware automated access of memory-mapped SPI 
memory disabled. 
  
  1 Hardware-automated access of memory-mapped SPI 
memory enabled. 
  
30 QSPI_MEMMAP_WR_ERR_MASK  Memory Mapped Write Error Mask. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MEMMAP_WR_ERR_MASK bit 
specifies whether an error response is returned to the 
fabric upon write attempts to address space reserved for 
memory-mapped reads of SPI memory. Whether or not a 
write error response is masked by this bit, the associated 
sticky notification bit ?.:QSPI_STATUS:MMWE? bit is still set. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Write error response returned upon write attempts to 
memory-mapped SPI memory. 
  
  1 Write error response masked (not returned) upon write 
attempts to memory-mapped SPI memory. 
  
29 QSPI_MEMMAP_RD_ERR_MASK  Memory Mapped Write Error Mask. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MEMMAP_RD_ERR_MASK bit 
specifies whether an error response is returned to the 
fabric upon write attempts to address space reserved for 
memory-mapped reads of SPI memory. Whether or not a 
write error response is masked by this bit, the associated 
sticky notification bit ?.:QSPI_STATUS:MMWE? bit is still set. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Write error response returned upon write attempts to 
memory-mapped SPI memory. 
  
  1 Write error response masked (not returned) upon write 
attempts to memory-mapped SPI memory. 
  
[28:23] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 

=== PDF PAGE 61 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 61 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
22 QSPI_MULTI_IO_ORDER  Start on MOSI. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MULTI_IO_ORDER bit is valid only 
when ?.:.:MIOM? is enabled for either DIOM or QIOM, and 
this bit selects the starting pin and the bit placement on 
pins for these modes. $[/p$]$[p$]In DIOM, by default 
(QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MULTI_IO_ORDER =0) SPI sends 
first bit on the DQ2_QMST  pin and second bit on the 
DQ1_QMST pin. In QIOM, by default, the QSPI sends first 
bit on the D3  pin, second bit on the D2  pin, third bit on 
the DQ2_QMST pin and fourth bit on the DQ1_QMST  pin. 
This order can be reversed by setting the 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MULTI_IO_ORDER bit. When this bit 
is set, the QSPI sends first bit on the DQ1_QMST pin. The 
first bit referred to here depends on the ?.:.:LSBF? bit 
setting (MSB bit or LSB bit). 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Start on MISO (DIOM) or start on SPIQ3 (QSPI).   
  1 Start on MOSI.   
[21:20] QSPI_MULTI_IO  Multiple I/O Mode. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MULTI_IO 
bits enable QSPI operation in dual I/O mode (DIOM) or 
quad I/O mode (QIOM). $[/p$]$[p$]These bits may only be 
changed when the QSPI is disabled (?.:.:EN? =0). 
0x0 R/W 
  00 No MIOM (disabled).   
  01 DIOM operation.   
  10 QIOM operation.   
  11 Reserved.   
19 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
18 QSPI_FAST_MODE  Fast-Mode Enable. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_FAST_MODE 
bit enables fast mode operation for QSPI receive transfers. 
QSPI transmit operations in fast mode are the same as 
normal mode. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable.   
  1 Enable.   
[17:16] QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_WATERMARK  Flow Control Watermark. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_WATERMARK bits 
select the watermark level of the transmit channel 
(QSPI_QSPI_TX_FIFO buffer) or receive channel 
(QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO buffer) that triggers flow control 
operation. These bits are applicable only when the QSPI is 
a slave (?.:.:MSTR? = 0) and flow control is enabled 
(?.:.:FCEN? =1). When the watermark condition is met, the 
QSPI slave de-asserts the RDY pin. 
0x0 R/W 
  00 TFIFO empty or RFIFO full.   
  01 TFIFO 75% or more empty, or RFIFO 75% or more full.   
  10 TFIFO 50% or more empty, or RFIFO 50% or more full.   
  11 Reserved.   
15 QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_POL  Flow Control Polarity. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_POL bit selects flow 
control polarity  for the RDY pin when flow control is 
enabled. When the RDY pin is active, the QSPI is indicating 
it's ready for data transfer. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Active-low RDY.   
  1 Active-high RDY.

ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 62 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
14 QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_CHSEL  Flow Control Channel Selection. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_CHSEL bit selects 
whether the QSPI applies flow control to the transmit 
channel (QSPI_QSPI_TX_FIFO buffer) or receive channel 
(QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO buffer). This bit is applicable only 
when the QSPI is a slave and flow control is enabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Flow control on RX buffer.   
  1 Flow control on TX buffer.   
13 QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_EN  Flow Control Enable. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_FLOW_CTRL_EN bit enables QSPI 
flow control operation, which permits slow slave devices 
to interface with fast master devices. This bit controls 
operation of the RDY pin. Note that options for flow 
control operation are available using the ?.:.:FCCH?, 
?.:.:FCPL?, and ?.:.:FCWM? bits. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable.   
  1 Enable.   
12 QSPI_LSB_FIRST  Least Significant Bit First. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_LSB_FIRST bit selects whether the 
QSPI transmits/receives data as LSB first (little endian) or 
MSB  first (big endian). This bit may only be changed when 
the QSPI is disabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 MSB sent/received first (big endian).   
  1 LSB sent/received first (little endian).   
11 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[10:9] QSPI_SIZE  Word Transfer Size. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_SIZE bits 
select the QSPI transfer word size as 8, 16 or 32 bits. To 
ensure correct operation, both the master and slave must 
be configured with the same word size. This bit may only 
be changed when the QSPI is disabled (?.:.:EN? =0). 
0x0 R/W 
  00 8-bit word.   
  01 16-bit word.   
  10 32-bit word.   
  11 Reserved.   
8 QSPI_MISO_EN  Enable MISO. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MISO_EN bit 
enables master-in-slave-out (MISO, DQ1_QMST) mode. 
This QSPI mode is applicable only when the QSPI is a slave. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable.   
  1 Enable.   
7 QSPI_CS_POL_IN_TRAN  Slave Select Polarity Between Transfers. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CS_POL_IN_TRAN bit selects the 
state (polarity) for the SELn pin in-between QSPI transfers 
when the QSPI is a master and hardware slave select 
assertion is enabled (?.:.:ASSEL? =1). In slave mode, this bit 
affects the detection of both transmit collision 
(?.:QSPI_STATUS:TC? and under-run (?.:QSPI_STATUS:TUR?) 
errors. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 De-assert slave select (high).   
  1 Assert slave select (low).   

=== PDF PAGE 63 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 63 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
6 QSPI_CS_CTRL  Slave Select Pin Control. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CS_CTRL bit selects whether the 
QSPI hardware sets the SS_QMSTn pin output value 
(ignoring the slave select ?.:QSPI_SLV_SEL:SSEL1? - 
?.:QSPI_SLV_SEL:SSEL7? bits) or whether software control 
of the slave select bits set the SS_QMSTn pin output value. 
This feature is applicable only when the QSPI is a master.  
When hardware control is enabled, the SS_QMSTn pin 
output is asserted during the transfers, and the pin 
polarity between transfers is selected by the ?.:.:SELST? bit.  
When software control is enabled, the SS_QMSTn pin 
output value is set through software control of the slave 
select bits, and as such, the pin may either remain asserted 
(low) or be deasserted between transfers. 
0x1 R/W 
  0 Software Slave Select Control.   
  1 Hardware Slave Select Control.   
5 QSPI_CPOL  Clock Polarity. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CPOL bit selects 
whether the QSPI uses an active-low or active-high signal 
for the QSPI clock (SCK_QMST). This bit works with the 
?.:.:CPHA? bit to select combinations of clock phase and 
polarity for the SCK_QMST pin. This bit may only be 
changed when the QSPI is disabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Active-high SPI CLK.   
  1 Active-low SPI CLK.   
4 QSPI_CPHA  Clock Phase. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CPHA bit selects 
whether the QSPI starts toggling the signal for the QSPI 
clock (SCK_QMST) from the start of the first data bit or 
from the middle of the first data bit. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CPHA bit works with the ?.:.:CPOL? 
bit to select combinations of clock phase and polarity for 
the SCK_QMST pin. This bit may only be changed when 
the QSPI is disabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 SPI CLK toggles from middle.   
  1 SPI CLK toggles from start.   
3 QSPI_OPEN_DRAIN  Open Drain Mode. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_OPEN_DRAIN bit configures the 
data output pins (DQ2_QMST and DQ1_QMST to behave 
as open drain outputs, which prevents contention and 
possible damage to pin drivers in multi-master or multi-
slave QSPI systems. $[/p$]$[p$]When 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_OPEN_DRAIN is enabled and the 
QSPI is a master, the QSPI three-states the DQ2_QMST pin 
when the data driven out on DQ2_QMST is a logic-high. 
The QSPI does not three-state the DQ2_QMST pin when 
the driven data is a logic-low. $[/p$]$[p$]When 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_OPEN_DRAIN is enabled and the 
QSPI is a slave, the QSPI three-states the DQ1_QMST pin 
when the data driven out on DQ1_QMST is a logic-high. 
$[/p$]$[p$]Note that an external pull-up resistor is 
required on both the DQ2_QMST and DQ1_QMST pins 
when QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_OPEN_DRAIN is enabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable.   
  1 Enable.   

=== PDF PAGE 64 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 64 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
2 QSPI_CS_PROT_EN  Protected Slave Select Enable. The 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CS_PROT_EN bit enables the 
SS_QMSTn pin to provide error detection input in a multi-
master environment when the QSPI is in master mode. If 
some other device in the system asserts the SS_QMSTn pin 
while QSPI is enabled as master (and 
QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_CS_PROT_EN is enabled), this 
condition causes a mode fault error. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable.   
  1 Enable.   
1 QSPI_MASTER  Master/Slave. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_MASTER bit 
toggles the QSPI between master mode and slave mode. 
This bit may only be changed when the QSPI is disabled. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Slave.   
  1 Master.   
0 QSPI_EN  Enable. The QSPI_QSPI_CTRL.QSPI_EN bit enables QSPI 
operation. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Disable SPI module.   
  1 Enable.   
 
RECEIVE CONTROL REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001008, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_RX_CTRL 
The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL register enables the QSPI receive channel, initiates receive transfers, and configures QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO 
buffer watermark settings. 
Table 56. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_RX_CTRL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:19] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[18:16] QSPI_RXFF_URG_WM  Receive FIFO Urgent Watermark. The 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXFF_URG_WM bits select the receive 
FIFO (QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO) watermark level for urgent data bus 
requests. The QSPI also uses this watermark level for generation of 
the ?.:QSPI_INT_LATCH:RUWM? interrupt. When an urgent 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO watermark is enabled with 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXFF_URG_WM, the ?.:.:RRWM? selection 
is used as the de-assertion condition for any 
?.:QSPI_INT_LATCH:RUWM? interrupts that are latched. 
0x0 R/W 
  000 Disabled.   
  001 25% full RFIFO.   
  010 50% full RFIFO.   
  011 75% full RFIFO.   
  100 Full RFIFO.   
  101 Reserved.   
  110 Reserved.   
  111 Reserved.   
[15:14] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 

=== PDF PAGE 65 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 65 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[13:12] QSPI_RXFF_REG_WM  Receive FIFO Regular Watermark. The 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXFF_REG_WM bits select the receive 
FIFO (QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO) watermark level for regular data bus 
requests. When an urgent QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO watermark is enabled 
with ?.:.:RUWM?, the QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXFF_REG_WM

0x0 R/W 
  00 Empty RFIFO.   
  01 RFIFO less than 25% full.   
  10 RFIFO less than 50% full.   
  11 RFIFO less than 75% full.   
[11:9] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
8 QSPI_RXD_OVRUN  Receive Data Overrun. The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXD_OVRUN 
bit selects handling for receive data requests when the receive buffer 
(QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO) is full. If enabled and QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO is 
full, the QSPI overwrites old data in the buffer with incoming data. If 
disabled and QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO is full, the QSPI keeps old data in 
the buffer and discards incoming data. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Discard incoming data if SPI_RFIFO is full.   
  1 Overwrite old data if SPI_RFIFO is full.   
7 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[6:4] QSPI_RXD_REQ  Receive Data Request. The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CTRL.QSPI_RXD_REQ bits 
select receive FIFO (QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO) watermark conditions that

RECEIVED WORD COUNT REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000101C, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_RX_CNT 
The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT register holds a count of the number of words remaining to be received by the QSPI. To start the decrement of 
the word count in QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT, enable the receive word counter (?.:QSPI_RX_CTRL:RWCEN? =1). The QSPI uses the word 
count to control the duration of transfers and to signal the completion of a burst of transfers with the receive finish interrupt 
(?.:QSPI_INT_LATCH:RF?). In DMA mode, the QSPI uses the QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT to ensure that the number of frames received 
during a DMA transfer is equal to the number of words programmed in the DMA channel controller. The values programmed into the 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT registers should match the word count in the DMA configuration. The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT maintains the 
number of frames to be received in a transfer. The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT should only be changed when the counter is disabled. 
Table 61. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_RX_CNT 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:16] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[15:0] QSPI_RX_CNT  Received Word Count. The QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT.QSPI_RX_CNT bits hold the 
receive transfer word count. 
0x0 R/W 
 
RECEIVED WORD COUNT RELOAD REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001020, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT 
The QSPI_QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT register holds the receive word count value that the QSPI loads into the QSPI_QSPI_RX_CNT register 
when the transfer count decrements to zero. To prevent the QSPI from reloading the counter, use zero for the reload count value. The 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT should only be changed when the counter is disabled. 
Table 62. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:16] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[15:0] QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT  Received Word Count Reload. The 
QSPI_QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT.QSPI_RX_RLD_CNT bits hold the receive 
transfer word count reload value. 
0x0 R/W 
 
TRANSMITTED WORD COUNT REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001024, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_TX_CNT 
The QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT register holds a count of the number of words remaining to be transmitted by the QSPI. To start the 
decrement of the word count in QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT, enable the transmit word counter (?.:QSPI_TX_CTRL:TWCEN? =1). The QSPI 
uses the word count to control the duration of transfers and to signal the completion of a burst of transfers with the transmit finish 
interrupt. In DMA mode, the QSPI uses the QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT to ensure that the number of frames transmitted during a DMA 
transfer is equal to the number of words programmed in the DMA channel controller. The values programmed into the 
QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT registers should match the word count in the DMA configuration. The QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT maintains the 
number of frames to be transmitted in a transfer. The QSPI_QSPI_TX_CNT should only be changed when the counter is disabled.

INTERRUPT MASK REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001030, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_INT_MASK 
The QSPI_QSPI_INT_MASK register unmasks (enables) or masks (disables) QSPI interrupts. When a condition is indicated by a bit in 
the QSPI_QSPI_STATUS register and the corresponding interrupt is unmasked in QSPI_QSPI_INT_MASK, the QSPI latches the 
interrupt’s bit in the QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH register, queuing the interrupt for service.

STATUS REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001040, Reset: 0x00440001, Name: QSPI_STATUS 
The QSPI_QSPI_STATUS register indicates QSPI status including FIFO status, error conditions, and interrupt conditions. When an 
interrupt condition from this register is unmasked (enabled) by the corresponding bit in the QSPI_QSPI_INT_MASK register, the 
interrupt is latched into the corresponding bit in the QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH register.

QSPI_QSPI_STATUS.QSPI_MEMMAP_FF_ERR bit =0 when a 1 is 
written to it. The QSPI_QSPI_STATUS.QSPI_MEMMAP_FF_ERR bit

effect. 
0x0 R/W1C

whether a slave has de-asserted the RDY pin to stall the QSPI

request. This bit is valid only when the QSPI is a master 
(?.:QSPI_CTRL:MSTR? =1 and flow control is enabled 
(?.:QSPI_CTRL:FCEN? =1). 
0x0 R 
  0 No Stall (RDY pin asserted).   
  1 Stall (RDY pin de-asserted).

MASKED INTERRUPT CONDITION REGISTER? 
Address: 0x40001044, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_INT_LATCH 
The QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH register latches interrupts, queuing the interrupts for service. When a condition is indicated by a bit in the 
QSPI_QSPI_STATUS register and the corresponding interrupt is unmasked in QSPI_QSPI_INT_MASK, the QSPI latches the interrupt’s 
bit in QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH.

The QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH_CLR register permits clearing individual mask bits in the QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH register without 
affecting other bits in the register. Use write-1-to-clear on a bit in QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH_CLR to clear the corresponding bit in the 
QSPI_QSPI_INT_LATCH register.

RECEIVE FIFO DATA REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001050, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_RX_FIFO 
The QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO register has an interface to the receive shift register in the QSPI and has an interface to the processor’s data 
buses. The top level of the buffer is visible to programs as the 32-bit QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO register, but the size (number of word 
locations) of the receive FIFO is actually flexible with transfer word size. The size of the receive FIFO is 8 if word size is 8-bit, or the size is 
4 if word size is 16-bit, or the size is 2 if word size is 32-bit.  
Both masters and slaves may stop or stall receive transfers based on FIFO status. When the receive FIFO is full, the QSPI master stops 
initiating new transfers on the QSPI if ?.:QSPI_RX_CTRL:RTI? is enabled. A slave may stall the QSPI interface when the content of the 
FIFO crosses the selected watermark. If data reception continues after QSPI_QSPI_RX_FIFO is full, the data in the receive FIFO is 
invalid, and the QSPI indicates this condition with receive overrun (?.:QSPI_STATUS:ROR?). This condition is possible when 
?.:QSPI_RX_CTRL:RTI? =0 and ?.:QSPI_RX_CTRL:REN? =1 for a master, or for a slave that does not exercise flow control.  
Note that the receive FIFO is reset (cleared) when the QSPI is disabled after being enabled. 
Table 71. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_RX_FIFO 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:0] QSPI_RX_DATA  Receive FIFO Data. 0x0 R 
 
TRANSMIT FIFO DATA REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001058, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_TX_FIFO 
The QSPI_QSPI_TX_FIFO register has an interface to the transmit shift register in the QSPI and has an interface to the processor’s data 
buses. The top level of the buffer is visible to programs as the 32-bit QSPI_QSPI_TX_FIFO register, but the size (number of word 
locations) of the transmit FIFO is actually flexible with transfer word size. The size of the transmit FIFO is 8 if word size is 8-bit, or the 
size is 4 if word size is 16-bit, or the size is 2 if word size is 32-bit.  

=== PDF PAGE 78 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 78 of 337 
Both masters and slaves may stop or stall transmit transfers based on FIFO status. When the transmit FIFO is empty, the QSPI master 
stops initiating new transfers on the QSPI if ?.:QSPI_TX_CTRL:TTI? is enabled. A slave may stall the QSPI interface when the content of 
the FIFO crosses the selected watermark. If data transmit requests continue after QSPI_QSPI_TX_FIFO is empty, the data sent from the 
transmit FIFO is invalid, and the QSPI indicates this condition with transmit underrun (?.:QSPI_STATUS:TUR?). This condition is 
possible when ?.:QSPI_TX_CTRL:TTI? =0 and ?.:QSPI_TX_CTRL:TEN? =1 for a master, or for a slave that does not exercise flow 
control.  
Note that the transmit FIFO is reset (cleared) when the QSPI is disabled after being enabled.

Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:0] QSPI_TX_DATA  Transmit FIFO Data. 0x0 R/W 
 
MEMORY MAPPED READ HEADER REGISTER 
Address: 0x40001060, Reset: 0x00000000, Name: QSPI_MMRD_HDR 
The QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR register enables the use of memory-mapped mode. This mode allows direct memory-mapped read 
accesses of a SPI memory device and is primarily used to directly execute instructions from a SPI FLASH memory without using a low-
level software driver. All overhead tasks such as transmission of the read header, pin turnaround timing and receive data sizing are 
handled in hardware. 
The memory-mapped access mode is enabled by setting the CTL.MMSE bit. The features within the QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR register 
include a command skip mode, variable length byte addressing, and independent multi-pin support for command transmission, address 
transmission and data reception. In addition the command op-code and mode bytes are fully programmable. 
Table 73. Bit Descriptions for QSPI_MMRD_HDR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:30] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
29 QSPI_MMRD_CMD_PINS  Pins Used for Command. The 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_CMD_PINS bit specifies 
the number of pins to be used for command transmission. 
This bit must be set consistent with expectations established 
by read opcode. Hardware does not interpret the OPCODE, 
but rather relies on this bit to specify behavior. When cleared, 
overrides ?.:QSPI_CTRL:MIOM? bits). When set, uses bits 
specified by ?.:QSPI_CTRL:MIOM? bit setting. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Use only one pin: MOSI (overrides ?.:QSPI_CTRL:MIOM? bits in 
register SPI_CTL). 
  
  1 Use pins specified by  ?.:QSPI_CTRL:MIOM? bits in register 
SPI_CTL. 
  
28 QSPI_MMRD_CMD_SKIP_EN  Command Skip Enable. The 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_CMD_SKIP_EN bit 
enables command skip mode where the address is sent first 
and the OPCODE field is not sent 
(QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_CMD_SKIP_EN bit =1). 
This mode is useful for supporting XIP (Execute-In-Place) 
operation where only the address is sent and the same read 
command is assumed. The SPI flash device must be primed 
with an initial read command, before the 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_CMD_SKIP_EN bit is 
set. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 OPCODE field is sent first followed by address.   
  1 OPCODE field is not sent; address is sent first.   

=== PDF PAGE 79 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual

Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
27 QSPI_MMRD_WRAP  SPI Memory Wrap Indicator. The 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_WRAP bit must be set 
by software if software places a connected SPI memory device 
into a 8-byte, 16-byte or 32-byte wrap mode based on the 
ILINE and DLINE field setting of the cache configuration 
register address wrap mode. Software achieves this by 
transmitting a vendor specified command to the SPI memory 
device while the ?.:QSPI_CTRL:MMSE? bit =0. $[/p$]$[p$]If the 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_WRAP bit =1, the SPI 
does not need to deassert the SPI slave select signal and 
resend the read header in order to wrap to the cache line base 
when servicing misaligned cache fill requests. Although this 
improves cache fill efficiency, it requires that the SPI deassert 
the SPI slave select pin and resend the read header whenever 
a DMA burst requests crosses 32 byte alignments. Setting this 
bit improves cache throughput but decreases DMA 
throughput. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 SPI Memory auto increments address purely sequentially.   
  1 SPI Memory auto increments address but wraps within 32 
Byte lines. 
  
26 QSPI_MMRD_MERGE_EN  Merge Enable. When the 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_MERGE_EN bit is set, 
SPI hardware combines the two successive transfers. This 
increases the throughput rate when accessing a large number 
of sequential memory locations. For more information refer to 
the Merged Read Accesses section. 
0x0 R/W 
[25:24] QSPI_MMRD_TRISTATE_DMY  Tristate Dummy Timing. These bits specify whether and when 
output pins are tristated during the interval of time specified 
by DMYSIZE.  Output pins potentially tristated include all pin 
which were used to transmit the address 
0x0 R/W 
  00 Tristate outputs immediately.   
  01 Tristate outputs after 4 bits of dummy/mode are transmitted.   
  10 Tristate outputs after 8 bits of dummy/mode are transmitted.   
  11 Never Tristate outputs never (previously specified output state 
is held). 
  
[23:16] QSPI_MMRD_MODE  Mode Field. These bits specify up to a leading byte to be 
transmitted during the interval of time specified by DMYSIZE.  
This first byte, or a portion of it, is interpreted as mode bits 
when certain opcodes are used in conjunction with certain 
SPI memory device.  Mode bits are sent using the same 
number of pins which were used to transmit the address.  
Once sent, output pins will be held in their final resultant state 
until the conclusion of all dummy byte periods, unless 
tristating of outputs is specified first by bits TRIDMY. 
0x0 R/W 
15 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[14:12] QSPI_MMRD_DMY_SIZE  Bytes of Dummy/Mode. These bits specify the number of

Dummy bytes elapse assuming dummy bits are transmitted 
using the same number of pins which were used to transmit 
address. 
0x0 R/W 
  000 0 Bytes.   
  001 1 Bytes.   
  010 2 Bytes.   
  011 3 Bytes.

[10:8] QSPI_MMRD_ADR_SIZE  Bytes of Read Address. The 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_ADR_SIZE bit field 
defines the number of bytes used to specify the read address. 
The read address is sent immediately following the 
transmission of opcode. Unlike opcode bits, address bits may 
be sent using either one or multiple pins. The number of pins 
is selected using the ?.:.:ADRPINS? bit. The address sent to a 
connected SPI memory device is an echo of the read address 
received by the SPI peripheral slave port. Least significant 
bytes of address are sent when entire address is not sent. 
0x0 R/W

[7:0] QSPI_MMRD_OP_CODE  Read Opcode. The 
QSPI_QSPI_MMRD_HDR.QSPI_MMRD_OP_CODE bit field 
specifies initial bits transmitted in response to a read request 
of SPI memory. Although any opcode may be sent, values 
0x03, 0x0B, 0x3B, 0x6B, 0xBB, and 0xEB are likely to be the 
most commonly used. Opcode is sent by the SPI without 
interpretation; the states of these bits have no affect beyond 
specifying what is initially shirted across the SPI interface. 
0x0 R/W

SOC HIGH PRIORITY LEVEL TRIGGER INTERRUPT CONNECTION REGISTER 
Address: 0x40002014, Reset: 0xFFFFFFFF , Name: SOC_HIPRI_LVLINT_CON 
This register is used for user to re-configure or update 'Level Sensitive' peripherals' interrupt priority levels. In default, 'Level Sensitive' 
peripherals' interrupts are connected at TDSP interrupt input pin 0~14, which are 'low priority' interrupt from TDSP perspective. TDSP 
input interrupt pin 16~23 are 'high priority' interrupt and are available to be connected with specific block interrupt sources.

Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access

LINE CONTROL REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000480C, Reset: 0x0000, Name: UART_LCR 
Table 91. Bit Descriptions for UART_LCR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:7] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
6 UART_BRK  Set Break. 0x0 R/W 
5 UART_SP  Stick Parity. Used to force parity to defined values. When set, the parity will 
be based on the following bit settings :  
UART_UART_LCR.UART_EPS = 1 and UART_UART_LCR.UART_PEN = 1, parity 
will be forced to 0. 
UART_UART_LCR.UART_EPS = 0 and UART_UART_LCR.UART_PEN = 1, parity 
will be forced to 1. 
UART_UART_LCR.UART_EPS = X and UART_UART_LCR.UART_PEN = 0, no 
parity will be transmitted 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Parity Will Not Be Forced Based on EPS and PEN. Parity Will Not Be Forced 
Based on Parity Select and Parity Enable Bits. 
  
  1 Parity Forced Based on Parity Select and Parity Enable Bits.   
4 UART_EPS  Parity Select. This bit only has meaning if parity is enabled 
(UART_UART_LCR.UART_PEN set). 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Odd Parity Will Be Transmitted and Checked.   
  1 Even Parity Will Be Transmitted and Checked.   
3 UART_PEN  Parity Enable. Used to control of the parity bit transmitted and checked. The 
value transmitted and the value checked will be based on the settings of 
UART_UART_LCR.UART_EPS and UART_UART_LCR.UART_SP . 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Parity Will Not Be Transmitted or Checked.   
  1 Parity Will Be Transmitted and Checked.   
2 UART_STOPPED  Stop Bit. Used to control  the number of stop bits transmitted. In all cases 
only the first stop bit will be evaluated on data received. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Send 1 Stop Bit Regardless of the Word Length Select Bit.   
  1 Word Length of Stop Bits. Send a Number of Stop Bits Based on the Word 
Length as Follows: WLS = 00, 1.5 Stop Bits Transmitted (5-bit Word Length) 
WLS = 01 or 10 or 11, 2 Stop Bits Transmitted (6 or 7 or 8-bit Word Length). 
  
[1:0] UART_WLS  Word Length Select. Selects the number of bits per transmission. 0x0 R/W 
  00 5 Bits.   
  01 6 Bits.   
  10 7 Bits.   
  11 8 Bits.

SCRATCH BUFFER REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000481C, Reset: 0x0000, Name: UART_SCR 
Table 95. Bit Descriptions for UART_SCR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:8] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[7:0] UART_SCR  Scratch. The scratch register is an 8-bit register used to store intermediate results. 
The value contained in Scratch Register does not affect UART functionality or 
performance. Only 8 bits of this register are implemented. Bits 15 to 8 are read 
only and always return 0x00 when read. Writable with any value from 0 to 255. A 
read will return the last value written. 
0x0 R/W 
 
FIFO CONTROL REGISTER 
Address: 0x40004820, Reset: 0x0000, Name: UART_FCR 
Table 96. Bit Descriptions for UART_FCR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:8] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[7:6] UART_RFTRIG  RX FIFO Trig Level. 0x0 R/W 
  00 1 Byte to Trig RX Interrupt.   
  01 4 Byte to Trig RX Interrupt.   
  10 8 Byte to Trig RX Interrupt.   
  11 14 Byte to Trig RX Interrupt.   
[5:4] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
3 UART_FDMAMD  FIFO DMA Mode. 0x0 R/W 
  0 In DMA Mode 0, Assert DMA Request. In DMA Mode 0, RX DMA Request Will 
Be Asserted Whenever There's Data in RBR or RX FIFO and De-assert 
Whenever RBR or RX FIFO is Empty; TX DMA Request Will Be Asserted 
Whenever THR or TX FIFO is Empty and De-assert Whenever Data Written 
to. 
  
  1 In DMA Mode 1, Assert DMA Request. In DMA Mode 1, RX DMA Request Will 
Be Asserted Whenever RX FIFO Trig Level or Time Out Reached and De-
assert Thereafter When RX FIFO is Empty; TX DMA Request Will Be Asserted 
Whenever TX FIFO is Empty and De-assert Thereafter When TX FIFO is 
Completely Filled up Full. 
  
2 UART_TFCLR  Clear TX FIFO. 0x0 W 
1 UART_RFCLR  Clear RX FIFO. 0x0 W

FRACTIONAL BAUD RATE REGISTER 
Address: 0x40004824, Reset: 0x0000, Name: UART_FBR 
Table 97. Bit Descriptions for UART_FBR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
15 UART_FBEN  Fractional Baud Rate Generator Enable. The generating of fractional baud rate 
can be described by the following formula and the final baud rate of UART 
operation is calculated as Baudrate = (UCLK / (2 * 
(UART_UART_FBR.UART_DIVM + UART_UART_FBR.UART_DIVN/2048) * 16 * 
UART_UART_DIV) ) 
0x0 R/W 
[14:13] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[12:11] UART_DIVM  Fractional Baud Rate M Divide Bits 1 to 3. This bit should not be 0 when 
Fractional Baud Rate enabled. 
0x0 R/W 
[10:0] UART_DIVN  Fractional Baud Rate N Divide Bits 0 to 2047. 0x0 R/W

value.

REGISTER DETAILS: GPT  
16-BIT LOAD VALUE REGISTER 
Address: 0x40006000, Reset: 0x0000, Name: GPT_LD_V AL 
Table 108. Bit Descriptions for GPT_LD_VAL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:0] GPT_LD_VAL  Load Value. The Up/Down counter is periodically loaded with this value if 
periodic mode is selected (GPT_CTRL[3]=1). LOAD writes during Up/Down 
counter timeout events are delayed until the event has passed. 
0x0 R/W 
 
16-BIT CURRENT TIMER VALUE REGISTER 
Address: 0x40006004, Reset: 0x0000, Name: GPT_CURR_V AL 
Table 109. Bit Descriptions for GPT_CURR_VAL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:0] GPT_CURR_VAL  Current Count. Reflects the current Up/Down counter value. Value delayed 
two PCLK cycles due to clock synchronizers. 
0x0 R 
 
CONTROL REGISTER 
Address: 0x40006008, Reset: 0x000A, Name: GPT_CTRL 
Table 110. Bit Descriptions for GPT_CTRL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:8] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
7 GPT_RLD  Reload Control. RLD is only used for periodic mode; this bit allows the user 
to select whether the Up/Down counter should be reset only on a timeout 
event or also when GPTCLRI[0] is set 
0x0 R/W 
  1 Resets the Up/down Counter When GPTCLRI[0] is Set.   
  0 Up/Down Counter is Only Reset on a Timeout Event.   
6 GPT_CLK_SEL  Clock Select. Used to select a timer clock from the two available clock 
sources 
0x0 R/W 
  0 PCLK.   
  1 32KHz Clock Generated from AON.   
5 RESERVED  Reserved 0x0 R 
4 GPT_EN  Timer Enable. Used to enable and disable the timer. Clearing this bit resets 
the timer, including the GPTVAL register. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 DIS. Timer is Disabled (Default).   
  1 EN. Timer is Enabled.   
3 GPT_MODE  Timer Mode. This bit is used to control whether the timer runs in periodic 
or free running mode. In periodic mode the up/down counter starts at the 
defined LOAD value (T0LD); in free running mode the up/down counter 
starts at 0x0000 or 0xFFFF depending on whether the timer is counting 
up or down. 
0x1 R/W 
  0 FREERUN.Timer Runs in Free Running Mode.   
  1 PERIODIC. Timer Runs in Periodic Mode (Default).   
2 GPT_UP_DOWN  Count up. Used to control whether the timer increments (counts up) or 
decrements (counts down) the Up/Down counter. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 DIS. Timer is Set to Count Down (Default).   
  1 EN. Timer is Set to Count up.   

=== PDF PAGE 98 ===
ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual UG-2257 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 98 of 337 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[1:0] GPT_PRE_SCALER  Prescaler. Controls the prescaler division factor applied to the timer's 
selected clock. If CLK source 0 or CLK Source 1 are selected then Prescaler 
value 0 means divide by 4, else it means divide by 1. 
0x2 R/W 
  00 Source_clock / [1 or 4].   
  01 Source_clock / 16.   
  10 Source_clock / 256.   
  11 Source_clock / 32,768.

STATUS REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000601C, Reset: 0x0000, Name: GPT_STATUS 
Table 112. Bit Descriptions for GPT_STATUS 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[15:8] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
7 GPT_STATUS_INT_CLR_DONE  GPTCLRI Synchronization. This bit is set automatically when 
the user sets GPTCLRI[0]=1. It is cleared automatically when 
the clear interrupt request has crossed clock domains and 
taken effect in the timer clock domain 
0x0 R 
  0 CLR. the Interrupt is Cleared in the Timer Clock Domain.   
  1 SET. GPTCLRI[0] is Being Updated in the Timer Clock Domain.   
6 GPT_STATUS_CTRL_BUSY  Timer Busy. This bit informs the user that a write to GPT_CTRL 
is still crossing into the timer clock domain. This bit should be 
checked after writing GPT_CTRL and further writes should be 
suppressed until this bit is cleared. 
0x0 R 
  0 Timer Ready to Receive Commands to GPT_CTRL.   
  1 Timer Not Ready to Receive Commands to GPT_CTRL.   
[5:1] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
0 GPT_STATUS_TMOUT  Timeout Event Occurred. This bit set automatically when the 
value of the counter reaches zero while counting down or 
reaches full scale when counting up. This bit is cleared when 
GPTCLRI[0] is set by the user. 
0x0 R 
  0 No Timeout Event Has Occurred.   
  1 a Timeout Event Has Occurred.

PLL AND PGA POWER CONTROLS REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C005, Reset: 0x02, Name: PLL_PGA_PWR 
Table 126. Bit Descriptions for PLL_PGA_PWR 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
6 PGA2_EN  Note that the PGA inverts the signal going through it. 0x0 R/W 
  0 PGA2 powered down.   
  1 PGA2 powered up with slewing.   
5 PGA1_EN  Note that the PGA inverts the signal going through it. 0x0 R/W 
  0 PGA1 powered down.   
  1 PGA1 powered up with slewing.   
4 PGA0_EN  Note that the PGA inverts the signal going through it. 0x0 R/W 
  0 PGA0 powered down.   
  1 PGA0 powered up with slewing.   
[3:2] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
1 XTAL_EN  Crystal Oscillator Enable. 0x1 R/W 
  0 Crystal oscillator powered off.   
  1 Crystal oscillator powered on.   
0 PLL_EN  PLL Enable. 0x0 R/W 
  0 PLL Powered Off.   
  1 PLL Powered On.

STATE RETENTION CONTROLS REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C00C, Reset: 0x01, Name: KEEP_CM 
Table 133. Bit Descriptions for KEEP_CM 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:1] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
0 CM_KEEP_ALIVE  Common Mode (CM) output keep alive during power down. 0x1 R/W 
  0 Common Mode (CM) output will turn off when PWR_MODE=2'b00. This 
allows for lower shutdown power, but longer start-up timing. 
  
  1 Common Mode (CM) output will stay on when PWR_MODE=2'b00. This 
allows for faster start-up timing, but larger shutdown power. 
  
 
RETENTION CONTROL FOR ADP AND SOC MEMORIES REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C00D, Reset: 0x00, Name: MEM_RETAIN 
Table 134. Bit Descriptions for MEM_RETAIN 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:2] SOC_MEM_RETAIN  State Retention control for SOC power domain memories. Bit[2]: Controls 
IRAM0 retention. Bit[3]: Controls DRAM0 retention. Bit[4]: Controls DRAM1 
retention. Bit[5]: Controls SYSRAM retention. Bit[6]: Controls FFSRAM0 
retention. Bit[7]: Controls FFSRAM1 retention. 1'b1 -- retention  1'b0 -- no 
retention 
0x0 R/W 
[1:0] ADP_MEM_RETAIN  State Retention control for ADP power domain memories. Bit[0]: controls 
FDSP and EQ instruction/ parameter memory retention. Bit[1]: controls 
FFSRAM2 memory retention. 1'b1 -- retention  1'b0 -- no retention 
0x0 R/W

at 24.576MHz, 49.152MHz, 73.728MHz, or 98.304MHz.

  1 Switch between NVM and LVM is  handled by internal FSM. 
When HP_LVMODE_HOTSW_MODE=0, playback path will be 
muted and then the HP power supply switches. When 
HP_LVMODE_HOTSW_MODE=1,the HP power supply switches 
between NVM and LVM when the output signal is crossing 
zero.

4 TDSP_WAIT_MODE_BYP  HiFi3z can enter sleep (wait) mode by WAITI instruction, this gate off

INPUT ASRC CONTROL, SOURCE, AND RATE SELECTION REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C0A0, Reset: 0x02, Name: ASRCI_CTRL 
Table 225. Bit Descriptions for ASRCI_CTRL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 ASRCI_MORE_FILT  Input ASRC Additional Filtering Enable. This can enable additional filtering 
within the ASRC which can provide higher performance under some 
conditions. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 No additional voice band filter.   
  1 Voice Band Filter On.   
6 ASRCI_VFILT  Input ASRC Voice Filter Enable. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Voice-Filter Off.   
  1 Voice-Filter On.   
5 ASRCI_LPM  Input ASRC Low Power Mode Selection. 0x0 R/W 
  0 High-Performance Mode.   
  1 Low-Power Mode.   
4 ASRCI_SOURCE  Input ASRC Source. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Serial Audio Port 0 source for Input ASRC.   
  1 Serial Audio Port 1 source for Input ASRC.   
3 ASRCI_LPM_II  Input ASRC Low Power Mode Selection II. Even lower power. 0x0 R/W 
  0 High-Performance Mode.   
  1 Low-Power Mode.   
[2:0] ASRCI_OUT_FS  Input ASRC Sample Rate Selection. 0x2 R/W 
  000 12kHz Sample Rate.   
  001 24kHz Sample Rate.   
  010 48kHz Sample Rate.   
  011 96kHz Sample Rate.   
  100 192kHz Sample Rate.

FASTDSP CURRENT BANK AND BANK RAMPING CONTROLS REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C0B1, Reset: 0x70, Name: FDSP_CTRL1 
Table 234. Bit Descriptions for FDSP_CTRL1 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:4] FDSP_RAMP_RATE  FastDSP Parameter Bank Ramp Rate of Change. Determines time to 
complete full ramp from one bank to another. 
0x7 R/W 
  0 0.02s Ramp.   
  1 0.04s Ramp.   
  2 0.06s Ramp.   
  3 0.08s Ramp.   
  4 0.1s Ramp.   
  5 0.15s Ramp.   
  6 0.2s Ramp.   
  7 0.25s Ramp.   
  8 0.3s Ramp.   
  9 0.5s Ramp.   
  10 0.75s Ramp.   
  11 1s Ramp.   
  12 1.25s Ramp.   
  13 1.5s Ramp.   
  14 1.75s Ramp.   
  15 2s Ramp.   
3 FDSP_ZERO_STATE  Zeroes the state of the FastDSP data memory during bank switching. 
When switching active parameter banks between two settings, zeroing 
the state of the bank prevents the new filter settings from being active 
on old data that is recirculating in filters. Zeroing the state may prevent 
filter instability or unwanted noises upon bank switching. 
0x0 R/W 
  0 Do not zero state during bank switch.   
  1 Zero state during back switch.   
2 FDSP_RAMP_MODE  FastDSP Parameter Bank Ramp Mode. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Parameters will linearly ramp when current bank is changed.   
  1 Parameters will instantly change when current bank is changed.   
[1:0] FDSP_BANK_SEL  FastDSP Current Parameter Bank Selection. 0x0 R/W 
  0 FDSP uses Parameter Bank A.   
  1 FDSP uses Parameter Bank B.   
  10 FDSP uses Parameter Bank C.   
 

=== PDF PAGE 179 ===
UG-2257 ADAU186x Hardware Reference Manual 
 
Rev. 0 | Page 179 of 337 
FASTDSP BANK RAMPING STOP POINT REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C0B2, Reset: 0x3F , Name: FDSP_CTRL2 
Table 235. Bit Descriptions for FDSP_CTRL2 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:6] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[5:0] FDSP_LAMBDA  FastDSP Bank Switch Ramp Stop Point. Lambda is a 6-Bit value representing 
the point along the linear interpolation curve between two banks at which 
the bank ramp switch will stop. 0=((63/64)*A + (1/64)*B), 1 =((62/64)*A + 
(2/64)*B), …, 62 = ((1/64)*A + (63/64)*B), 63=B (default) Lambda can be 
updated “on the fly” via control interface. In order to complete a bank switch a 
value of 63 (default setting) must be set. Actual current ramp point 
(current_lambda : 0-63) can be read via a status register. When this reaches 63 
the bank switch is complete and current parameters used will match current 
bank. Actual step size of linear interpolation will be ~12-bits (4096 steps) 
Parameters in banks that are being ramped between should not change 
during a bank switch 
0x3F R/W 
  0 Bank switch parameter ramp will stop at 1/64 of full ramp.   
  1 Bank switch parameter ramp will stop at 2/64 of full ramp.   
  2-61 …   
  62 Bank switch parameter ramp will stop at 63/64 of full ramp.   
  63 Bank switch parameter ramp will complete ramp to current bank.

Table 263. Bit Descriptions for FDSP_SL_UPDATE

[7:1] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
0 FDSP_SL_UPDATE  FastDSP Safeload Update. Writing a 1 to this register will write the 
parameter values in the FDSP_SL_Px registers to the addresses in the 
current bank associated with the instruction number in the 
FDSP_SL_ADDR register at the beginning of the next frame. 
0x0 W 
  0 No Action.   
  1 Writing of 1 will cause update of safeload parameters at the beginning of 
next frame.

  0 tri-state disabled.   
[5:4] SPT0_SLOT_WIDTH  Serial port - selects slot width. 0x0 R/W 
  00 32 BCLK's per slot.

  001 Left Justified,delay by 0.   
  000 Typical I2S mode, delay by 1.   
  010 Delay by 8.   
  011 Delay by 12.   
  100 Delay by 16.   
0 SPT0_SAI_MODE  Serial port - selects SAI mode. 0x0 R/W 
  0 STEREO (I2S, LJ, RJ).   
  1 TDM.

SERIAL PORT 1 CONTROL 1 REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C0F3, Reset: 0x00, Name: SPT1_CTRL1 
Table 295. Bit Descriptions for SPT1_CTRL1 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R/W 
6 SPT1_TRI_STATE  Serial port output - tri-state enable. 0x0 R/W 
  1 tri-state enabled.   
  0 tri-state disabled.   
[5:4] SPT1_SLOT_WIDTH  Serial port - selects slot width. 0x0 R/W 
  00 32 BCLK's per slot.   
  01 16 BCLK's per slot.   
  10 24 BCLK's per slot.   
[3:1] SPT1_DATA_FORMAT  Serial port - selects data format. 0x0 R/W 
  001 Left Justified,delay by 0.   
  000 Typical I2S mode, delay by 1.   
  010 Delay by 8.   
  011 Delay by 12.   
  100 Delay by 16.   
0 SPT1_SAI_MODE  Serial port - selects SAI mode. 0x0 R/W 
  0 STEREO (I2S, LJ, RJ).   
  1 TDM.   
 
SERIAL PORT 1 CONTROL 2 REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C0F4, Reset: 0x00, Name: SPT1_CTRL2 
Table 296. Bit Descriptions for SPT1_CTRL2 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:4] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
3 SPT1_BCLK_POL  Serial port - selects bclk polarity. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Capture on rising edge.   
  1 Capture on falling egde.   
[2:0] SPT1_BCLK_SRC  Serial port - selects bclk source and rate. 0x0 R/W 
  000 BCLK is from external source.   
  001 Generate BCLK at 3.072MHz.   
  010 Generate BCLK at 6.144MHz.   
  011 Generate BCLK at 12.288MHz.   
  100 Generate BCLK at 24.576MHz.

Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 PDM_MORE_FILT  PDM Output Additional Interpolation Filtering Selection. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Less interpolation filtering : Lower delay.   
  1 More interpolation filtering : Higher delay.   
[6:5] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
4 PDM_RATE  PDM Output Rate. 0x0 R/W 
  0 6.144MHz PDM Output Rate.   
  1 3.072MHz PDM Output Rate.

  0 High frequency response not compensated (lower delay).   
  1 High frequency response compensated for samples rates of 192kHz or 
lower when DAC_MORE_FILT=1. (higher delay).

  000 12kHz Sample Rate.   
  001 24kHz Sample Rate.   
  010 48kHz Sample Rate.

  0 Volume change occurs at any time.   
  1 Volume change only occurs at zero crossing.

  0 Soft Volume Ramping.   
  1 Hard/Immediate Volume Change.   
0 PDM_VOL_LINK  PDM Output Volume Link. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Each ADC channel uses its respective volume value.   
  1 All ADC channels use Channel 0 volume value.

IRQ SIGNALING AND CLEARING REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C150, Reset: 0x00, Name: IRQ_CTRL1 
Table 366. Bit Descriptions for IRQ_CTRL1 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 IRQ4_PINOUT_POL  IRQ4 pin output polarity control. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Active Low Interrupt Signaling.   
  1 Active High Interrupt Signaling.   
6 IRQ3_PINOUT_POL  IRQ3 pin output polarity control. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Active Low Interrupt Signaling.   
  1 Active High Interrupt Signaling.   
5 IRQ2_PINOUT_POL  IRQ2 pin output polarity control. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Active Low Interrupt Signaling.   
  1 Active High Interrupt Signaling.   
4 IRQ1_PINOUT_POL  IRQ1 pin output polarity control. 0x0 R/W 
  0 Active Low Interrupt Signaling on Pin.   
  1 Active High Interrupt Signaling on Pin.   
3 IRQ4_CLEAR  Write one to clear IRQ4. 0x0 R/W1T 
  0 Not Clear.   
  1 Write once to clear IRQ4.   
2 IRQ3_CLEAR  Write one to clear IRQ 3. 0x0 R/W1T 
  0 Not Clear.   
  1 Write once to clear IRQ4.   
1 IRQ2_CLEAR  Write one to clear IRQ 2. 0x0 R/W1T 
  0 Not Clear.   
  1 Write once to clear IRQ2.   
0 IRQ1_CLEAR  Write once to clear IRQ 1. 0x0 R/W1T 
  0 Not Clear.   
  1 Write once to clear IRQ1.

IRQ1 MASKING REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C151, Reset: 0x7F , Name: IRQ1_MASK1 
Table 367. Bit Descriptions for IRQ1_MASK1 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
7 RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
6 IRQ1_AVDD_UVW_MASK  Mask AVDD undervoltage warning to IRQ1. 0x1 R/W

FASTDSP CURRENT LAMBDA REGISTER 
Address: 0x4000C400, Reset: 0x3F , Name: READ_LAMBDA 
Table 404. Bit Descriptions for READ_LAMBDA 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[7:6] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
[5:0] FDSP_CURRENT_LAMBDA  FastDSP Bank Switch Ramp Current Lambda Status. Lambda is a 6-
Bit value representing the point along the linear interpolation 
curve between two banks at which the bank ramp switch will stop. 
0=((63/64)*A + (1/64)*B), 1 =((62/64)*A + (2/64)*B), …, 62 = 
((1/64)*A + (63/64)*B), 63=B (default) Lambda can be updated “on 
the fly” via control interface. In order to complete a bank switch a 
value of 63 (default setting) must be set. Actual current ramp point 
(current_lambda : 0-63) can be read via a status register. When this 
reaches 63 the bank switch is complete and current parameters 
used will match current bank. Actual step size of linear 
interpolation will be ~12-bits (4096 steps) Parameters in banks that 
are being ramped between should not change during a bank 
switch 
0x3F R 
  0 Bank switch parameter ramp is at 1/64 of full ramp.   
  1 Bank switch parameter ramp is at 2/64 of full ramp.   
  … …   
  62 Bank switch parameter ramp is at 63/64 of full ramp.   
  63 Bank switch parameter ramp is complete.

Address: 0x40040300, Reset: 0x000001FF, Name: DS_CTRL 
Table 539. Bit Descriptions for DS_CTRL 
Bits Bit Name Settings Description Reset Access 
[31:25] RESERVED  Reserved. 0x0 R 
24 LT_EN  Enable TIE lookup table interface. 0 is disable; 1 is enable. 0x0 R/W 
[23:16] LT_RDY_WIDTH  Data Ready Signal Width (Unit is Core Clock Period) for TIE Lookup table 
Reading. When it is zero, data ready signal will be auto clear through 
reading dedicated address of lookup table. 
0x0 R/W 
[15:0] RDY_RATE_DIV  Rate Division for Generating Ready Signal from 24M clock in Data Sync. 
Default is for 48K. 
0x1FF R/W

ESD Caution  
ESD (electrostatic discharge) sensitive device. Charged devices and circuit boards can discharge without detection. Although this product features patented or proprietary protection 
circuitry, damage may occur on devices subjected to high energy ESD. Therefore, proper ESD precautions should be taken to avoid performance degradation or loss of functionality.
